# EZ Draft architecture conventions

Written by EZD-000 (vault/plans/EZD-000.md). Every build session reads it by section, never whole (R26 R11; EZD-244): the core sections on the line below, then the sections its ticket lists (the `Architecture:` line of `node vault/tools/next_ticket.mjs --reads <ticket> <role>`). Heading texts are unique and hold no `'` or `;`; renaming a heading means updating every ticket list that names it (`--check` names them). It does not repeat CLAUDE.md; the build rules stay there (Q-0005). Sources are cited in short form: `02` = vault/spec/sections/02-product-overview.md, `03` = 03-data-model.md, `11` = 11-non-functional-requirements.md, `R24 #n` / `R26 Rn` = vault/spec/decisions/13a, 13b, `Q-nnnn` = vault/questions/QUESTIONS.md, QUESTIONS-answered.md or QUESTIONS-archive.md (one ledger in three files; look in all three). The vault copy is `vault/architecture.md`, re-made by the orchestrator on every merge that changes this file (Q-0012 (b)).

Core sections: Folder layout; Tests; Test scope (Q-0006); Errors and logging; Adding a dependency

## Runtime

- Node.js 22, the major `.github/workflows/ci.yml` installs (ci.yml is changed only by Josh). Vercel Functions use the same major (EZD-003 sets it). The root `package.json` `engines.node` is `22.x`.
- Browser floor: Safari and iOS Safari 16.4+, Chrome and Edge 111+, Firefox 113+ (Q-0013 (a), vault/spec/decisions/02-product-overview.md; EZD-010 relies on Firefox 113 for `oklch()`, Q-0026 (a)). No polyfills and no `browserslist` override.
- pnpm: the version in the `pnpm` row of Dependencies, pinned again in the root `package.json` `packageManager` field (check C1 keeps the two equal). pnpm reads that field and runs the pinned version, so the machine's installed pnpm (CI: `npm install -g pnpm`) does not matter.

### Hosting

R24 #1; 02 Stack Hosting; Q-0113; Q-0116 (a); Q-0123 (b); Q-0106; EZD-003.

- Vercel project `ezdraft`, Root Directory `app`. Config is `app/vercel.json` (framework `nextjs`, function region `iad1`). Node `22.x` comes from `app/package.json` `engines`. pnpm runs through Corepack (`ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_COREPACK=1`) and the root `packageManager` pin.
- Production branch `main`: production changes only when a phase PR merges into `main`. Until launch each merge goes live at once through Auto-assign, on the `ezdraft-dev` database (Q-0264); from launch a build goes live only when EZD-023b's release workflow promotes it.
- Previews only for the phase PR, read as a pull request from `develop` (Vercel names no target branch), through the Ignored Build Step in the Vercel project; a pull request from any other branch builds nothing. A plain `develop` push builds no preview; the gate's empty `chore: preview` commit after the PR opens gives the PR its preview. Previews use the `ezdraft-dev` database (its values entered in Vercel `Preview` on 2026-10-21); until launch Production uses it too (Q-0264).
- Domain `ezdraft.ezcontractorbinder.com`. DNS is at Spaceship; Vercel provides the HTTPS certificate and the HTTP to HTTPS redirect. "Auto-assign Custom Production Domains" is on until launch, so the address serves each phase merge (Q-0264); Josh turns it off at launch (runbook Before launch checklist item 2) and it stays off, so from then on the address shows only a build the release workflow promotes (the release refuses to run while it is on, `vercel_auto_assign_on`).
- Rollback is Vercel's previous deployment.
- The live check `scripts/hosting/check-live.mjs` is never run by the gate. EZD-023b's release workflow runs its checks after every release; anyone may run it by hand (read-only).
- Agents never change Vercel or DNS settings; those steps are in `docs/runbook.md` for Josh.

Which project's values go where: local is the local stack; preview is the dev project (11 Operations: preview deploys use the dev database); until launch production is the dev project too, so the live address runs on the `ezdraft-dev` database (Q-0264); from launch production is the production project, which Josh sets up from the Before-launch checklist (items 1 and 4; Q-0113). Josh enters the values in Vercel by hand from `docs/runbook.md` (Vercel and the subdomain, step 3; Before launch checklist, item 4); agents never change Vercel settings.

### Email (EZD-008)

Q-0035; Q-0036; Q-0041; R24 #15; EZD-008a; EZD-008b.

- Resend through `app/src/server/email/` (`sendEmail`, EZD-008a); one recipient per message; server-only, never imported by browser code (11 Budgets, 200 KB; Server-only data access and can()).
- Transport by `EMAIL_TRANSPORT` (`stub` or `resend`), no default; both selectable per environment (Q-0041); the stub is refused in production; unit tests read `readStubOutbox()`, browser tests read `EMAIL_STUB_DIR` (EZD-008a). The stub module is loaded with import() only when the stub transport sends, so the production path never loads it (EZD-036).
- **Sender (Q-0036):** emails sent for a company pass `company: { name: tenant.name, replyTo: tenant.contact.email ?? null }`; the From shows "<Company> via EZ Draft" and replies go to the company email from company settings (S15a Contact), never to the person who pressed Send. Emails no company sends omit `company` and show `EMAIL_FROM` as is.
- **Copy link (Q-0035; R24 #15):** every feature that sends an email shows the person who sent it the link from `SendEmailResult.link`, with a Copy link button, whether the send succeeded or failed, because emails land in spam. Each sending ticket's plan names where its Copy link appears and tests it. **Exception: sign-in emails** (EZD-007) never show their link on screen, since anyone who typed an email address would get in; the sign-in page tells the user to check spam and accepts the 6-digit code (Q-0035 orchestrator note; R26 R15). Emails no person sends from a screen (EZD-024's alert emails, Q-0067 (b)) have no sender screen; EZD-024's plan records how it meets the rule.
- Sign-in emails are sent by Supabase Auth through Resend SMTP (runbook `## Email (EZD-008)` step 6), not through `sendEmail`.
- Live check `scripts/email/send-test.mjs`, run by Josh by hand only, refused under `CI`, never by the gate (Q-0153).
- Agents never change Resend, DNS, Vercel or Supabase settings; those steps are in `docs/runbook.md` (CLAUDE.md rule 3; Q-0003: the Resend account is shared with EZ Binder).
- **Input checks (EZD-151):** `sendEmail` refuses (`EmailInputError`) a subject holding U+0000–U+001F, U+007F, U+0085, U+2028 or U+2029; a caller that builds a subject from free text passes it through `toSubjectLine()` first (owner alerts do). A company name loses those characters. With `VERCEL_ENV` set to any value the link must be `https:`; locally and in tests `http:` is allowed. The stub keeps at most `STUB_OUTBOX_MAX` (100) messages in memory and drops the oldest; the `EMAIL_STUB_DIR` files are not capped.
- **Sender copy (Q-0701; EZD-389):** every `sendEmail` call passes `sentBy`: the acting user's sign-in email as `{ email }`, or `null` when no person's action sends the email (owner alerts, notification emails). When `senderCopyOn()` in `app/src/server/email/sender-copy.ts` is true (the one switch, `SENDER_COPY_ON`), `sentBy` is not null, its address differs from `to` ignoring case and the recipient's send succeeded, `sendEmail` sends a second message to `sentBy.email` alone: the same From, Reply-To, link and `lang`, subject `office.email.copy_subject`, first paragraph `office.email.copy_note`, idempotency key `<key>:copy` when the call has a key. A failed or throwing copy leaves `SendEmailResult` as it is and goes to Sentry as `email_copy_failed` with its cause code only.

### Background jobs (EZD-009)

EZD-009 (vault/plans/EZD-009.md Steps 1-9); 02 Stack Background jobs; 03 Tables job; 11 Operations, rules 3, 10, 11; P4; Q-0027 (a); Q-0059 (a); Q-0031 (a).

- **Table `public.job`** (`<ts>_create_job.sql`): `tenant_id`, `project_id` (null = a tenant-level job), `kind` (`import`, `export`, `render`, `ai_read`, `thumbnail`, `diff`, `cleanup`, `test`, `notification_email` (EZD-110a); `JOB_KINDS` in `app/src/lib/jobs/kinds.ts` holds the same list), `payload`, `status` (`queued`, `running`, `done`, `failed`), `progress` (whole percent), `attempts`, `max_attempts` (1-5), `error`, `run_after`, the lease `run_id` and `lease_expires_at`, the split cursor `state`, `result`, `created_by`, timestamps. `payload`, `state` and `result` are JSON objects of at most 16,384 bytes. One select policy (EZD-006 template; a tenant-level job is visible to all-projects members only); `authenticated` has `select` only, `service_role` all four.
- **No money in job rows:** every member who can see the project can select its jobs, so `payload`, `state` and `result` hold ids, small values and Storage paths only, never money or secrets (03 rule 2; R26 R2). A handler writes money to its own tables and puts ids or paths in the result. `error` holds a stable code (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$`), never a message, stack or provider text.
- **Enqueue:** a server route calls `enqueueJob(sessionClient, { kind, projectId, payload, runAfter? })` (`app/src/server/jobs/index.ts`), which checks the payload with the kind's schema, its size and its Storage paths, calls `public.enqueue_job` under the user's session (security definer, `authenticated` only; tenant from the claim; project scope or all-projects for a tenant-level job, else `42501`) and then `kickJobRunner()`, which registers `after(() => runJobs())` from `next/server`, so the run starts after the response is sent. The route checks permissions with `can()` first. Enqueuing routes keep the default `maxDuration`, because the kicked run lives in that function.
- **Sweep:** the Vercel Cron entry in `app/vercel.json` calls `GET /api/jobs/sweep` once a minute with `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` (`401 not_authorized` otherwise, compared in constant time); it runs `runJobs()` and answers `{ ran }`. Crons run on production deployments only, so previews pick up retries and dead runs only when another enqueue starts a run.
- **Claim, lease, fencing:** `public.job_claim(run_id, lease_seconds)` takes one due job with `for update skip locked`, sets `status` `running`, a new `run_id`, `lease_expires_at = now() + lease` and `attempts + 1`; it also takes a `running` job whose lease expired while `attempts < max_attempts` (error `lease_expired`). `job_fail_expired` fails expired jobs with no tries left. `job_progress`, `job_complete`, `job_continue` and `job_fail` write only where `run_id` still matches and return false otherwise; a run that gets false stops touching the job. The six `job_*` functions are invoker functions executable by `service_role` only, called through the secret-key client `createJobsDbClient()` (`app/src/server/jobs/db.ts`). All times come from the database clock.
- **Attempts, retry, backoff (Q-0059 (a)):** `attempts` goes up at claim, so a run that dies still counts. A handler error is retried while `attempts < max_attempts`, waiting `min(baseMs x 2^(attempts - 1), capMs)`: up to 5 tries, waiting 1, 2, 4 and 8 minutes (`baseMs` 60,000, `capMs` 480,000); a handler may set a lower `maxAttempts`. Nothing starts a run at `run_after`, so a wait rounds up to the next sweep. Never retried: `invalid_payload`, `invalid_result`, `no_handler`, a `JobError` with `retry: false`; a plain throw is `handler_failed` (retried); a dead run is `lease_expired`. `onJobFailed(job)` is called once per job that reaches `failed` (EZD-024 hooks in there).
- **Split rule:** a handler reads `ctx.timeLeft()` and returns `{ continue: <state> }` when the next unit will not fit; the job goes back to `queued` with its cursor and `attempts` 0, and the next run carries on. A handler always does at least one unit per slice.
- **Time values** (`app/src/server/jobs/config.ts`): `JOB_FUNCTION_MAX_SECONDS` 300 (Vercel Pro default, Q-0027 (a); Vercel docs 2026-10-24, R-027: 300 s default, 800 s maximum, 2 GB / 1 vCPU; the sweep route's literal `maxDuration`), `JOB_LEASE_SECONDS` 360 (limit + 60), `JOB_RUN_BUDGET_MS` 270,000 ((limit - 30) x 1000), `JOB_MIN_CLAIM_MS` 10,000.
- **Handler contract** (`app/src/server/jobs/registry.ts`; handlers registered in `getHandlers()`, `handlers.ts`): handlers are safe to run twice. Output paths are fixed by job id (`<tenant_id>/<project_id or tenant>/<job_id>/out/...` in the `jobs` bucket); an outside call that costs money or sends something carries an idempotency key made from the job id and the cursor. A handler runs with the secret key, which bypasses RLS, so it filters every query by `ctx.job.tenant_id` (and `project_id`). Every registered kind has an `expectSafeToRunTwice('<kind>', ...)` test (`app/test-harness/jobs/run-twice.ts`). The `test` kind is registered only outside production.
- **Progress:** the trigger `job_broadcast` pushes `job.progress` `{ jobId, kind, status, progress, attempts }` on `project:<project_id>` on each insert and each status or progress change (a failed send never rolls back the job write); tenant-level jobs push nothing. In the browser, `watchJob({ channel, jobId, fetchJob, onChange })` (`app/src/lib/jobs/watch-job.ts`) passes on every event for the job and fetches `GET /api/jobs/<id>` once when the job first finishes and once per resync; no timers, no polling (11 rule 11).
- **Read route:** `GET /api/jobs/<id>` returns `{ job: { id, projectId, kind, status, progress, attempts, error, result, createdAt, updatedAt, finishedAt } }` through the session client after `requireApiPermission('view_projects')` (401 `not_signed_in`, 403 `permission_denied`); a job on a project outside the member's listed projects is `404 job_not_found`; not a UUID or not visible is `404 job_not_found`.
- **Tests:** every test that calls `runJobs` against the database passes `onlyTenantId` = its own fresh tenant. Every test file that inserts or enqueues `job` rows holds `holdJobQueueShared()` (`app/test-harness/jobs/queue-lock.ts`) from the start of its `beforeAll` to the end of its `afterAll`, after deleting its jobs; the unscoped sweep tests hold `holdJobQueueExclusive()`. The test page is `/dev/jobs` (not found in production); its enqueue route `POST /api/dev/jobs` needs `edit_model` on the project (403 `permission_denied`, 404 `project_not_found`; EZD-145, Q-0279 (b)).

### AI (EZD-021)

EZD-021 (vault/plans/EZD-021.md Steps 1-6); 02 Stack AI, Background jobs; 07 External services; 03 rule 2; R24 #18; Q-0016; Q-0059 (a); Q-0076; Q-0077; Q-0027 (a).

- AI runs in jobs, with one exception. `app/src/server/ai/` is imported only by `app/src/server/jobs/**` and `app/src/server/assistant/**` (and tests), never a `'use client'` file (T19). `app/src/server/ai/` (`index.ts`) exports `callClaude(ctx, req, opts?)`, `callClaudeJson(ctx, req, schema, opts?)` and `editImage(ctx, req, opts?)`; each takes the job context (`AiJobContext`: `job`, `signal`, `timeLeft`, `sleep`); T19 is `scripts/ai/__tests__/ai-static.test.mjs`. The assistant code checks a user's own key with `checkAnthropicKey(key)` (EZD-5601); it never reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Claude purposes run in `ai_read` jobs; image edits for renders will run in `render` jobs (EZD-1101, EZD-1103).
- **Claude** through `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (`providers/anthropic.ts`, the only file that imports it), with the key, the address `https://api.anthropic.com`, `maxRetries: 0` (the queue retries) and the call's time budget always passed, and the key sent in the `x-api-key` header. Model aliases (`CLAUDE_MODELS`, `config.ts`): `opus` = `claude-opus-5-5`, `sonnet` = `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `haiku` = `claude-haiku-5-5`; each purpose ticket picks its alias. `callClaudeJson` strips one surrounding Markdown code fence, parses JSON and checks it with the given schema; any failure is `ai_bad_response`.
- **Image edits** over plain HTTP, one input image and an instruction (Q-0016: Gemini image, GPT Image 2, Flux Kontext pro): `gemini` (`POST <base>/v1beta/models/<model>:generateContent`, `x-goog-api-key`), `openai` (`POST https://api.openai.com/v1/images/edits`, multipart, `Authorization: Bearer`), `bfl` (`POST <base>/v1/<model>` with `x-key`, then the returned `polling_url` every 1,000 ms through `ctx.sleep` until `Ready`, then the `sample` without the key). The caller passes the provider model id. EZD-1103 builds the render adapter interface and the model registry on top.
- **Mode** (`getAiMode`, read per call; no default): `AI_MODE` unset or not `stub` / `live` is `ai_mode_not_set`; `stub` with `VERCEL_ENV=production` is `ai_stub_not_allowed`; `stub` runs the real adapter with the key `stub-key` and a recorded fetch (real keys are never read); `live` reads the provider's key (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `BFL_API_KEY`) and the global `fetch` at call time; a missing or blank key is `ai_key_not_set`, message "AI key not set", and fails only the jobs that need that provider. Local and tests `stub`; Preview `stub` by default, `live` when Josh sets it (Q-0077); Production `live`. Keys and base URLs are named in `app/src` only in `app/src/server/ai/config.ts`.
- **Configurable addresses (Q-0076).** `GEMINI_BASE_URL` and `BFL_BASE_URL` (`getProviderBaseUrl`, read per call in both modes): unset or blank is the default (`https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, `https://api.bfl.ai`); otherwise `https:` with no user name, password, query or fragment, one trailing `/` removed; anything else is `ai_base_url_invalid` and nothing is sent. Anthropic and OpenAI keep fixed addresses.
- **BFL host rule.** `polling_url` and `sample` come from the provider's answer, so they must be `https:` on `bfl.ai` or a subdomain of it, or on exactly the host of the BFL base URL; else `ai_bad_response` and no request goes to that URL (the key never leaves for a host a response names). Polling stops with `ai_provider_timeout` when `ctx.timeLeft()` is 1,000 ms or less.
- **Time.** Each call's budget is `min(ctx.timeLeft(), JOB_RUN_BUDGET_MS)`; its signal is the job's signal combined with a timeout of that budget, so no provider call outlives the job slice; running out is `ai_provider_timeout`.
- **Errors** (`AiError extends JobError`, `errors.ts`; `job.error` holds the code only; `message` is a fixed English text per code, never a provider body, prompt, image or key):

| Code | Retry | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ai_mode_not_set`, `ai_stub_not_allowed`, `ai_key_not_set`, `ai_base_url_invalid` | no | configuration |
| `ai_invalid_request` | no | the request fails its schema (`validate.ts`); nothing is sent |
| `ai_key_rejected` | no | HTTP 401 or 403 |
| `ai_provider_rejected` | no | any other 4xx except 429 |
| `ai_rate_limited` | yes | 429 |
| `ai_provider_unavailable` | yes | 5xx (Anthropic 529 included), network error, BFL `Error` / `Failed` |
| `ai_provider_timeout` | yes | the call's time ran out |
| `ai_no_output` | no | no text or image in the answer (refusal, Gemini with no image part, BFL `Request Moderated` / `Content Moderated`) |
| `ai_bad_response` | no | malformed body, JSON helper failure, BFL URL refused by the host rule |
| `ai_stub_no_recording`, `ai_stub_mismatch` | no | stub mode: no recording, or a request the recording does not expect |

- **No dollar cost and no money in job rows.** The client returns provider usage only (tokens, images); callers turn it into dollars (EZD-1101, EZD-1103; EZD-031 for the trial). Prices are entered by the owner (S15f, EZD-1102).
- **Reruns cost money.** Stub results are the same on every run; in live mode a rerun of a handler repeats the provider call and may pay twice. Handlers that store outputs (EZD-1101, EZD-1103) check for an existing output by job id before calling.
- **`ai_read` jobs** (`app/src/server/jobs/handlers/ai-read.ts`, registered in `getHandlers()` in production too): payload `{ purpose, input }`, checked against the purpose's input schema, so `enqueueJob` refuses an unknown purpose or bad input (`invalid_payload`); result `{ purpose, output }`, checked against the purpose's output schema (`invalid_result`). EZD-009's default tries apply (Q-0059 (a): up to 5, waiting 1, 2, 4 and 8 minutes); `AiError.retry` stops every failure a later try cannot fix. Results must fit 16,384 bytes; large outputs go to Storage.
- **Adding a purpose.** Write `app/src/server/jobs/ai-purposes/<name>.ts` (`AiPurpose`: `purpose`, `input`, `output`, `run(ctx, input)`), add it to `getAiPurposes()` (`ai-purposes/index.ts`; test purposes only outside production, as `test.echo`), add its recording `"<provider>/<purpose>"` under `app/src/server/ai/test-harness/recordings/`, and test it on the stub (an `expectSafeToRunTwice('ai_read', …)` call covers the kind).

### Environments and releases (EZD-023b)

EZD-023b, EZD-023c, EZD-023d, EZD-023e (vault/plans/EZD-023e.md Steps 1-5; the archived EZD-023b plan, Steps 1-11); 11 Operations; R26 R12; Q-0106; Q-0112; Q-0113; Q-0116 (a); Q-0157; Q-0158 (a).

| Environment | Database | App | Env values | Changed by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| local | Local Supabase in Docker (`pnpm db:up`; each gate run wipes it, EZD-004a) | `next dev` and tests | `pnpm db:env`; email stub (EZD-008) | Agents and tests |
| dev | Supabase project `ezdraft-dev` in EZ Binder's Supabase organization (Q-0024 (a)); made-up test data only, its password used nowhere else (Q-0110) | Vercel Preview of the phase pull request (Q-0116 (a)); a plain `develop` push builds no preview; until launch also Vercel `Production` at `ezdraft.ezcontractorbinder.com` (Q-0264), so phase field checks use the preview before the phase merge and the live address after it; from launch the Preview only | Vercel `Preview` values = the dev project's values; `EMAIL_TRANSPORT` `stub` by default (Q-0041); until launch Vercel `Production` also holds the dev project's Supabase values (Q-0264); from launch only `Preview` does | Migrations through `supabase-dev.yml` on each `develop` push that changes `supabase/migrations/**`, or by Run workflow (Q-0107), with its secret in the GitHub environment `dev` (EZD-004c) |
| production | Supabase project `ezdraft-prod` in the same organization (Q-0024 (a)), created from the Before-launch checklist (Q-0113); until launch Vercel `Production` runs on `ezdraft-dev` (Q-0264) | Vercel `Production` at `ezdraft.ezcontractorbinder.com` (R24 #1); live since 2026-10-24, each phase merge going live through Auto-assign until launch (Q-0264); from the first real release only builds the release promotes | Vercel `Production` values = the prod project's values from launch (runbook Before launch item 4); until then the dev project's values (Q-0264) | From launch only `.github/workflows/release.yml`, when Josh merges a pull request into `main` (Q-0106); until launch each merge into `main` goes live through Auto-assign (Q-0264) |

- **Go-live choice** (archived Step 2): "Auto-assign Custom Production Domains" was off from EZD-003 and is on since 2026-10-24 until launch, so each merge into `main` goes live on the address at once, on the `ezdraft-dev` database, with no dump and no release (Q-0264). Josh turns it off at launch (runbook Before launch checklist item 2) and it stays off: each merge into `main` still gets a production build but the domain moves to it only when the release promotes it through the Vercel API, after the dump and the migrations. With auto-assign on, new code could serve before its migrations, and a failed dump or migration would leave the new build live (Q-0106; 11 Operations: "production is updated only by promoting a tagged build"). The release refuses to run while it is on (`vercel_auto_assign_on`).
- **Workflow** `.github/workflows/release.yml` (EZD-023e Step 2): trigger `push` to `main` only (Josh's merge is the only approval, Q-0106; a failed run is retried with Re-run jobs); top-level `permissions: {}`, job permissions exactly `contents: write` (the tag), `pull-requests: read` (the merged pull request), `actions: read` (the environment's branch rule); `concurrency` group `release-production`, never cancelled; `ubuntu-latest`, 45 minutes. Steps: checkout (`fetch-depth: 0`, `persist-credentials: false`), setup-node 22 with no cache, `npm install -g pnpm@<packageManager version>`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (Q-0158 (a): dependency build scripts stay denied by `allowBuilds`), then `node scripts/release/release.mjs`. Secrets are named only in the release step's `env:` (`GITHUB_TOKEN` plus the 10 `RELEASE_SECRET_NAMES` of `scripts/release/context.mjs`). Every `uses:` is pinned to a commit SHA; no `${{` in a `run:` line, no artifact, no cache.
- **Environment `release`** (archived Step 4; Q-0112 finding 2): every production secret lives in the GitHub environment `release`, deployment branch `main` only, administrators cannot bypass; the release checks that rule on every run (`environment_unprotected`). It is not Vercel's `Production` environment: the Vercel GitHub integration made that one for its deployment statuses, with no branch rule and administrators allowed to bypass, and GitHub environment names are case-insensitive, so `production` would be the same environment (archived "Environment name"). Never a repository or organization secret. EZD-025's backups use the same environment.
- **Until launch** a merge releases nothing: the `release` environment holds no secrets, so the run ends `not_configured` and is green (Q-0113). The merge still goes live: with Auto-assign on (Go-live choice above), Vercel puts its production build on the address at once, running on the `ezdraft-dev` database, with no dump and no tag (Q-0264). From launch a build goes live only when the release promotes it.
- **Tag format** (`### Release safety (EZD-023a)`): `phase-<N>` or `phase-<N>.<k>`. Naming rule (EZD-023b Step 2, `scripts/release/release-name.mjs`): a release tag already on the commit is a re-run (`resume`); else a `Phase N:` pull request title whose `phase-N` does not exist gives `phase-N` when newer than every tag (else `not_newer`); else the next `.k` of the highest tag; with no tag and no `Phase N:` title, `no_release_name`.
- **Step order.** 1 context (`agent_refused`, `not_in_actions`, `wrong_event`, `not_main`); 2 presence and masking (`not_configured`, `env_missing`); 3 the commit; 4 tip of `origin/main` (`not_latest`); 5 the merged pull request (`no_merged_pr`); 6 the environment rule (`environment_unprotected`); 7 the release name (`not_newer`, `no_release_name`); 8 EZD-023a's migration check with EZD-153's scratch database against the previous tag (`migration_check_failed`); 9 the Vercel project, token and the READY production build of the commit (`vercel_auto_assign_on`, `deployment_protection_off`, `vercel_api_failed`, `build_failed`, `no_build`); 10 Vercel values (`env_mismatch`). Then, unless that build is already live: A the commit's worktree and B tenant 1 and the timeout probe (`pending_check_failed`, `tenant_check_failed`, `migrate_timeouts_not_applied`, `owner_email_missing`, `bootstrap_file_invalid`); C the pending migrations; D the dump, off-site (`dump_failed`, `dump_upload_failed`); E the migrations, under EZD-241's release guard when there is a previous release tag (`release_guard_failed`, `release_guard_remove_failed`, `migrate_failed`, `release_guard_lost`); F tenant 1 on the first release (`bootstrap_failed`). G the tag (skipped on a re-run; `tag_failed`), then promote this commit's build (`promote_failed`); H verify: `/api/health` every 5 s for up to 120 s with this commit's newest migration as `schema_version`, then EZD-003's live check (`verify_failed`, with automatic rollback); I `release.json` next to the dump. Success is `released`; a crash of the CLI is `internal_error`. Notices (the run goes on): `vercel_token_expiring`, `vercel_token_no_expiry`, `vercel_token_expiry_unknown`, `owner_email_secret_unused`, `previous_deployment_unknown`. The CLI exits 0 only on `released` or `not_configured`.
- **Dumps** go to the B2 bucket under `releases/<tag>-<time>/` (roles, schema, data, `manifest.json`, `release.json`) with the release key, which can write only there; Object Lock keeps them 91 days (Q-0111) and the lifecycle rule deletes them after 13 weeks (Q-0075). Not a workflow artifact: the repository is public, artifacts of a public repository can be downloaded by anyone who can see the run, and dumps hold client data (archived Step 6 step 13).
- **Tenant 1** (archived Step 9): on the first release the release fills `supabase/bootstrap/tenant-1.sql` from the commit with `TENANT1_OWNER_EMAIL` and runs it through `psql` on stdin, with the connection in `PG*` environment variables and never in a psql argument (EZD-041), after the migrations and before the build goes live; nothing is written to disk or printed. Later releases skip it, and while `TENANT1_OWNER_EMAIL` is still set every summary carries `owner_email_secret_unused`. psql runs with `-v VERBOSITY=terse` (`PSQL_ARGS`), so it prints only severity and message lines; a bootstrap that ends without `ezd_bootstrap_done` logs psql's last stderr lines (at most 20) through `safeLines` (EZD-152).
- **Rollback:** automatic when the checks after going live fail (the previous production build is promoted again), or Vercel Instant Rollback by hand; the database is never rolled back (R26 R12; 11 Operations). Steps for Josh: `docs/runbook.md` `## Environments and releases (EZD-023)`.
- **Database password to the Supabase CLI** (EZD-042; Q-0262 (a)): `supabase db dump` and `supabase db push` get `--db-url` without the password; `buildDumpInvocation` and `buildPushInvocation` return `{ args, env }` and the password goes only into the child's `PGPASSWORD`, which the CLI reads under the URL's values and passes to pg_dump's container as an env value (R-016). `createSupabaseCli` drops every inherited `PG*` name first. psql gets the connection the same way (EZD-041). The dev migration push (`scripts/db/dev-push.mjs`, EZD-146) uses the same `push`; its child env carries the password only as `PGPASSWORD` and never `SUPABASE_DEV_DB_URL`. `scripts/release/__tests__/cli-push-local.test.mjs` proves on the local stack that CLI 2.121.0 reads `PGPASSWORD` and that the session applying a migration has the URL's `lock_timeout` and `statement_timeout`; whether Supabase's Session pooler passes them on is not proven there (R-019; the release's step B probe fails closed with `migrate_timeouts_not_applied`).
- **Release guard (EZD-241; Q-0358 (c); R-029).** EZD-153's scratch database holds no rows, so a drop or rename that runs only when production holds certain rows never runs there. With a previous release tag, step E first runs `createReleaseGuard({ sql }).install` (`scripts/release/release-guard.mjs`): it removes any guard left by a killed run, proves over the migration URL that the session setting `ezd.release_guard=on` reaches the session (`setting_not_applied`), reads production's `SNAPSHOT_SQL` (`snapshot_unreadable`), and installs schema `ezd_release_guard` (the protected list in the body of `protected()`, never a table, so a dump holds no guard rows; sequences `seen` and `applied`), the event triggers `ezd_release_guard_sql_drop` (`sql_drop`) and `ezd_release_guard_ddl_end` (`ddl_command_end`, renames and `set schema`) and the row trigger `ezd_release_guard_migration_row` on the CLI's history table `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`, all three `enable always` (`install_failed`). An install that fails and whose cleanup also fails is `release_guard_remove_failed`: no migration ran, the guard objects remain, and the next run's install removes them first. Protected: every snapshot object of production at the start of step E except those the base tag's `supabase/retired.json` retires (`isRetired`, a retired table covers its columns), matched by object id and name, checked per DDL statement; objects this release creates are free. The triggers act only when `current_setting('ezd.release_guard', true)` is `on`, which only step E's push URL sets (`-c ezd.release_guard=on` in `options`, after the timeouts; `withTimeouts(dbUrl, timeouts, settings)`, names and values checked, else `invalid_setting`); a refused statement raises `ezd_release_guard: <kind> <name> is dropped or renamed while <tag> still reads it` and the push fails with `migrate_failed`. The row trigger counts every history row the CLI inserts (`seen`) and those inserted with the setting on (`applied`); the CLI inserts one per applied migration in the push session, and sequences are not rolled back. After the push, whether it worked or failed, `remove` reads the enabled guard triggers and the two counts, then drops the triggers and the schema (the schema drop takes the row trigger with it). A failed remove (`remove_failed`), fewer than 3 enabled triggers (`guard_lost`), `seen` not equal to `applied`, or a good push whose `applied` is not step C's pending count (`guard_inactive`) is `release_guard_lost`, which outranks `migrate_failed`: nothing goes live, and the summary says not to press Re-run jobs, since a re-run finds nothing pending and would put the build live. A missing `releaseGuard` dependency with a base tag is `release_guard_failed` (`not_wired`) before the push. No base tag: no guard, no setting. The dev migration push (`scripts/db/dev-push.mjs`) uses no guard. Whether `postgres` may add event triggers on the cloud project is Josh's paste of `supabase/checks/release-guard-probe.sql` (`docs/runbook.md` `## Cloud checks (EZD-171)` J5), which always ends in an error so nothing it creates is kept.

### Off-site backups and restore (EZD-025)

EZD-025 (vault/plans/EZD-025.md Steps 1-8); 11 Data retention, Operations; R26 R13; Q-0074 (a); Q-0075; Q-0106; Q-0110; Q-0111; Q-0112; Q-0113.

- **Design.** Two scheduled GitHub Actions workflows on `main` copy production to a bucket at a second provider: every night `pnpm backup:files` copies the Storage objects changed since the last successful run, and every week `pnpm backup:db` writes a database dump. Before writing anything each run refuses unless the bucket has versioning on and Object Lock set. Each run is a row in the tenant-less table `backup_run` (EZD-135's System status page reads it). `pnpm backup:restore` takes the newest complete dump, builds a scratch local Supabase from the repo's migrations up to the dump's migration version, loads `data.sql`, uploads the copied files and checks that the named projects, their files and the migration version are back.
- **Backups are not `job` rows** (EZD-009): every `job` needs a `tenant_id` and a backup covers every tenant, a Vercel Function has no `pg_dump`, and the copy would have to fit the 300 s function limit (Q-0027 (a)).
- **Provider:** Backblaze B2 through its S3-compatible API (Q-0074 (a)), with `@aws-sdk/client-s3` and the client settings of `scripts/release/offsite-upload.mjs` (`forcePathStyle`, checksums `WHEN_REQUIRED`, `ContentMD5` on every PUT; errors reduced to the operation, key and status). The bucket is private with SSE-B2 (Q-0112 finding 10).
- **Workflows.** `.github/workflows/backup-files.yml` ("Off-site backup: files", cron `0 7 * * *`, 120 minutes) and `.github/workflows/backup-db.yml` ("Off-site backup: database", cron `0 8 * * 0`, 60 minutes); triggers `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` only; job `if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`, `environment: release`, permissions exactly `contents: read` and `actions: read`, concurrency per workflow never cancelled; steps as `release.yml` (pinned checkout with `persist-credentials: false`, setup-node 22 with no cache, `npm install -g pnpm@12.14.0`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`), then the script. Secrets are named only in the script step's `env:`: `backup-files.yml` the 8 names `BACKUP_SOURCE_DB_URL`, `BACKUP_SOURCE_SUPABASE_URL`, `BACKUP_SOURCE_SECRET_KEY`, `BACKUP_S3_ENDPOINT`, `BACKUP_S3_REGION`, `BACKUP_S3_BUCKET`, `BACKUP_S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `BACKUP_S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`; `backup-db.yml` the 6 without the two Storage names; both also `GITHUB_TOKEN` from `github.token`. They never name `VERCEL_*`, `TENANT1_OWNER_EMAIL` or `RELEASE_S3_*`, and the release never names the backup key or the two Storage names.
- **One environment, `release`** (Q-0106), shared with the release workflow and not Vercel's `Production`: `main` only, administrators cannot bypass. In GitHub Actions each CLI first checks presence, then that rule (`environment_unprotected`, `scripts/backup/environment.mjs` over `evaluateEnvironmentRule`), before any value is read or anything connects (Q-0112 finding 2). With every name of its list empty a run prints `backup not configured` and exits 0 (`not_configured`, green until launch, Q-0113); with some missing it exits 1 (`env_missing`). Production only: dev holds made-up data (Q-0110).
- **The restore key never goes to GitHub.** Only the B2 backup key (list, read, write; no `deleteFiles`, no `bypassGovernance`) is in `release`. The restore key (list and read only) lives in Josh's restore file outside the repo, read only with `--env-file <absolute path>`; its values go to the run, never into `process.env`.
- **Off-site key layout:** `files/<bucket_id>/<object name>` (metadata `source-etag`, `source-updated-at`, `sha256`) and `db/<yyyyMMdd'T'HHmmss'Z'>/{roles.sql, schema.sql, data.sql, manifest.json}`; `manifest.json` (`{ format: 1, created_at, source_ref, newest_migration, files: [{ name, size, sha256 }] }`) is uploaded last, so a folder without it is incomplete and never restored. EZD-023b's `releases/<tag>-<time>/` dumps carry the same manifest.
- **Preflight:** versioning must be `Enabled` (`versioning_off`) and Object Lock enabled with default retention `GOVERNANCE` of at least 91 days (`object_lock_off`, Q-0111); a refused run records one failed `backup_run` row and writes nothing.
- **Files cursor:** a run copies objects whose `storage.objects.updated_at` is newer than the last successful run's `files_cursor` minus a 3,600 s overlap (all with `--full` or no earlier success), keyset by `(updated_at, id)`; it skips a copy whose `source-etag` and size already match; `files_cursor` keeps the largest `updated_at` to the microsecond (sent as text, since a JavaScript Date holds milliseconds). `--only-bucket` limits a run (tests) and gives it its own scope `buckets:<ids>`.
- **No deletes off-site.** Code never deletes an off-site version; a source delete becomes a delete marker (`hide`, `DeleteObject` without a version id), and B2 lifecycle rules remove replaced or deleted file versions after 90 days and dumps after 13 weeks (Q-0075). The `source_empty` guard: when the source lists no object in scope while off-site has some, nothing is hidden and the run fails. The restore picks per key the current version, or the newest version before a delete marker (`pickRestoreVersions`, `scripts/backup/pick.mjs`), so a deleted project comes back within 90 days.
- **Dumps** are EZD-023d's `dumpDatabase`: the data dump leaves out `auth.sessions`, `auth.refresh_tokens`, `auth.one_time_tokens` and `auth.flow_state`, and a role password in `roles.sql` fails the run (Q-0112 finding 11).
- **Logs carry counts and codes only:** one JSON line per run (`{ ok, kind, code?, copied, skipped, hidden, bytes }` for files, `{ ok, kind: 'db_dump', code?, location?, bytes }` for the dump), never object names, ids, URLs or values; a failed dump's child lines pass only through `safeLines` over every configured value. Dumps never become artifacts. Both psql runners (`PSQL_ARGS`, `scratchPsqlArgs`) pass `-v VERBOSITY=terse`, so psql never writes `DETAIL:`, `HINT:`, `QUERY:`, `CONTEXT:`, `LINE n:` or a caret line; as the backstop, `safeLines` drops everything from the first `DETAIL:`, `CONTEXT:`, `QUERY:` or `LINE n:` line to the end of the output (its caret and continuation lines and any `HINT:` after it included, since a continuation line is client text and can start with any label; a `HINT:` before any such line is kept) and replaces the quoted value after the first `: ` of an `ERROR:` line with `"<value>"`, dropping the rest when the quote does not close on that line (EZD-152; Q-0288 (a)).
- **Scratch stack** (`scripts/backup/scratch/supabase/config.toml`): project `ezdraft-restore`, API `25321`, DB `25322`, shadow `25320`, pooler `25329` (below 32768, outside the outgoing port ranges; EZD-325), so it runs beside EZD-004a's stack; seed off; Studio, mail, edge runtime and analytics off; Realtime on, because EZD-018's migrations use `realtime.messages`. The schema comes from the repo's migrations up to the dump's `newest_migration` (refused with `repo_older_than_dump` when the repo lacks that version), not from the dump, so `supabase_migrations` and the `sent/` trigger are the repo's. `data.sql` is loaded as `supabase_admin` in one transaction with `session_replication_role = replica` after truncating the tables it names; files are uploaded through scratch Storage with the trigger off, then on again.
- **Restore** (`scripts/backup/restore.mjs`): `--project <uuid>` (repeatable), `--dump <db/… or releases/… prefix>`, `--from-dir <folder>` (a dump on disk; with `release.json` and no manifest the migration version comes from `git ls-tree` at its tag), `--keep` (leave the scratch stack running), `--record` (one `restore_drill` row in the source database), `--local`, `--env-file`. Refusals: `usage`, `no_dump`, `dump_corrupt` (SHA-256 against the manifest), `repo_older_than_dump`; checks: `migrations_mismatch`, `trigger_disabled`, `project_missing`, `file_missing`, `file_mismatch`, with `orphan_rows` counted. Guards, only with `--env-file`, in order: `agent_refused` (`CLAUDECODE`), `ci_refused` (`CI` or `GITHUB_ACTIONS`), `tty_required`, `env_file_not_absolute`, `env_file_in_repo`. Steps for Josh: `docs/runbook.md` `## Off-site backups and restore drill (EZD-025)`.
- **Backup check** (EZD-1704; Q-0232): `pnpm backup:check` (`scripts/backup/check.mjs`, flags `--local`, `--env-file`, `--only-bucket`) reads the newest succeeded `files` and `db_dump` rows of `backup_run` and exits 1 with `backup_stale` unless the file copy finished less than 26 hours ago and the dump less than 8 days ago (`evaluateBackupCurrency`, `FILES_MAX_AGE_MS`, `DB_DUMP_MAX_AGE_MS`); one JSON line, codes and times only; run by hand in the drill and every day by `.github/workflows/backup-check.yml` ("Off-site backup: check", cron `0 10 * * *`, 15 minutes; Q-0301 (b)): same shape as `backup-db.yml` (`schedule` and `workflow_dispatch`, `main` only, `environment: release`, `contents: read` and `actions: read`, pinned steps, no cache), its script step naming only `BACKUP_SOURCE_DB_URL` plus `GITHUB_TOKEN` from `github.token`. In Actions the check follows `db.mjs`: presence (`not_configured`, exit 0, green until launch, Q-0113), then the `release` environment rule (`environment_unprotected`), then the read; a red run is GitHub's failure email to Josh.

### DXF (EZD-029)

EZD-029 (vault/plans/EZD-029.md Steps 2-8); 02 Stack DXF in/out; 08 Imports, Architect drawings; 11 Budgets, rules 3 and 7; R26 R16; Q-0027 (a). Results: `spikes/EZD-029-dxf/out/results.json` (written DXF: `spikes/EZD-029-dxf/out/room-written.dxf`).

- **Packages.** Parser `dxf-parser` 1.1.2, with the spike's own HATCH handler (1.1.2 ships none) registered through `registerEntityHandler` (`spikes/EZD-029-dxf/src/hatch-handler.mjs`; EZD-901a carries it). Writer `@tarikjabiri/dxf` 2.9.0. In Node ESM the parser class is the default import (the package's UMD bundle sets `module.exports`; the named `DxfParser` import is undefined at run time).
- **Counting method** (R26 R16): an own raw scanner counts the entity records of the file by type, layer, space and block, with no parser; not read = raw count - parsed count, per type, for ENTITIES and BLOCKS separately and then combined. `VERTEX`, `ATTRIB` and `SEQEND` are sub-records of the record before them.
- **Required types** (08 Architect drawings) and whether the parser reads them, on the generated stand-in:

| 08 term | DXF entity types | Read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| linework | LINE, ARC, CIRCLE, LWPOLYLINE, POLYLINE, ELLIPSE, SPLINE | yes (POLYLINE on the synthetic fixture) |
| blocks | INSERT, plus the entities inside BLOCKS definitions | yes |
| hatches | HATCH | yes, through the own handler |
| text | TEXT, MTEXT | yes |
| dims | DIMENSION | yes |
| model and paper space, layouts | the paper-space flag (group 67) on entities | yes (`inPaperSpace`) |
| layers | the LAYER table, and group 8 on every entity | yes |

- **Time and memory** against the Q-0027 (a) limits (300 s, 2048 MB), each parse in a child Node process with `--max-old-space-size=2048`, killed after 300 s (Node v22.23.3, darwin; not the platform, EZD-901a proves the real run): synthetic fixture 22 of 22 entities read, 1.4 ms, peak 49.8 MB; the 150,000-entity stand-in (16.4 MB, plus 43 block entities) all read, 436 ms (parse 281 ms), peak 401 MB. Both `fitsOneSlice` (within the EZD-009 run budget, (300 - 30) x 1000 ms); a parse is one synchronous unit and cannot be split across slices.
- **Where parsing runs:** background jobs only (EZD-302; EZD-901a "the browser never parses DXF text"; 11 rule 3).
- **Decoding rule:** the file is read as latin1 to find `$ACADVER`; `AC1021` (R2007) and later are decoded as UTF-8, earlier versions stay latin1. Other code pages are EZD-901a's. A file starting `AutoCAD Binary DXF` is reported as binary and not parsed (S11 States "Unreadable file").
- **Node built-ins** (for EZD-020 and EZD-913): neither installed package imports a Node built-in (`require` or `import` of any `module.builtinModules` name, scanned in the installed files).
- **Writer** (for EZD-913 and EZD-911): writes `$ACADVER` `AC1021`, `$INSUNITS` 1, named layers, LINE, ARC, closed LWPOLYLINE, TEXT and a SOLID HATCH, read back in full by the parser, including the layer `Z-MARKUP-WP`. Recorded only (`spikes/EZD-029-dxf/src/write-room.mjs`): every written entity carries a handle (group 5); a LINE written with a colour carries group 62, and one written with a line weight carries no group 370. Not written or read back, only seen in the package's API: `addBlock` and `addInsert`, `addLinearDim`, `addPaperSpace` and 82 predefined hatch patterns besides SOLID; EZD-913 and EZD-911 prove what they use.

### Drawing list (EZD-238)

EZD-238 (vault/plans/EZD-238.md); 02 PDF out; 09h; Q-0507; Q-0508; Q-0574; Q-0575.

- **Files.** `packages/geometry/src/drawing/`: `build.ts` (`buildDrawingList`, `DrawingListOptions`: `roomAreaSqFt` gives the room label's area, EZD-235), `hatch.ts` (`hatchSegments`), `text.ts` (`measureTextPt`, `DRAWING_FONTS`), `types.ts` (item types, `drawingViewSchema`, `dimensionInputSchema`), `font-metrics.generated.ts` (`DM_MONO_400_METRICS`); all exported from `@ezdraft/geometry`.
- **Units.** Model inches, y up; angles in degrees, counter-clockwise from +x; text size and width in points. One floor per list (`view.level`); objects and dimensions on other floors make no items.
- **Layers.** Walls and openings on their `status` (`existing`, `demo`, `new`); rooms on `rooms`, room labels (`NAME · N SF`, Instrument Sans 600) included; dimensions on `dimensions`. An opening off every wall (`hostWallId` null) is drawn where it stands on `flagged` (EZD-204b).
- **Roles.** `cut` (wall faces and jambs, door leaf), `beyond` (window lines, room hatch), `hidden` (door swing arc), `dimension` (dimension lines). The list carries no colours or line widths.
- **Door swing and hand** (EZD-204b; Q-0642): each door is drawn from its own `hand` (hinge end) and `swing` (the side it opens to).
- **Font table.** Text is measured from the committed DM Mono 400 and Instrument Sans 600 advance tables (`DM_MONO_400_METRICS`, `INSTRUMENT_SANS_600_METRICS`, one generated file); no font file is read at run time. Regenerate with `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/geometry fonts:metrics` (reads `app/src/styles/fonts/dm-mono-latin-400-normal.woff2` and `app/src/styles/fonts/instrument-sans-latin-600-normal.woff2` with `fontkit`).

## Adding a dependency

CLAUDE.md rule 7 and 02 Named tools. A commit that adds or upgrades a package in any workspace `package.json` adds or edits its Dependencies row in the same commit. One version per package across the repo. Use an already listed package for a job before adding another. Internal packages are referenced as `workspace:*` and are not listed. Install with `pnpm add` (the workspace file sets `saveExact`, so the specifier is written exact). Check C1 enforces the row and the exact version; the code-reviewer fails a ticket without the row (CLAUDE.md rule 7).

Dependency build scripts (Q-0140 (a)): pnpm 12 refuses to install while any dependency's install script is unsettled. Every reported script is denied in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `allowBuilds` (map form, `<package>: false`); no entry is set to allow and no allow-all setting is used. The pnpm `permissions` setting (pnpm 12.11; its `build` capability overrides `allowBuilds`) is never used (EZD-148). Denied so far: `unrs-resolver` (pulled in by `eslint-config-next` through `eslint-import-resolver-typescript`; added by EZD-001), `esbuild` (pulled in by `tsx`; its install script only checks the platform binary that `optionalDependencies` already install; added by EZD-031). A new build script a ticket's install reports is denied the same way; if the build or tests need it, the builder stops and raises a question naming the package, what the script does, what fails without it and the effect on EZD-023b T17. No agent allows one on its own.

Settings from environment names (Q-0283 (a); EZD-177): pnpm 12 also reads its settings from `PNPM_CONFIG_*` and `pnpm_config_*` environment names (pnpm 12.14.0 CHANGELOG, 12.8.2 and 12.3.0 entries), so `PNPM_CONFIG_DANGEROUSLY_ALLOW_ALL_BUILDS=true` would run new build scripts past `allowBuilds`. No workflow file and no workspace `package.json` script names `pnpm_config_` in any letter case: `ci.test.mjs` rule (l) and `tooling.test.mjs` EZD-177 T2 refuse it. A name built at run time is checked by review.

Build flags (Q-0316 (a); EZD-182): pnpm 12 command-line flags also turn build scripts on past `allowBuilds`: `--dangerously-allow-all-builds`, `--config.<setting>` (the dot form, any setting) and `--allow-build <name>` (pnpm 12.14.0 `pnpm install --help`). No workflow line, workspace `package.json` script or `app/vercel.json` string value passes one, in any letter case: `ci.test.mjs` rule (m), `tooling.test.mjs` EZD-182 T2 and `hosting.test.mjs` EZD-182 T3 refuse them. The bare `--config <file>` of Vitest and Playwright stays allowed; a flag built at run time is checked by review.

Node built-in modules (REMEDIATION-PLAN 2.10; D1 (a), Josh 2026-11-01): a `node:` module is not a package and has no row above. The build records use `node:sqlite` (Node 22), one local file `~/.ezdraft/records.db` written by `scripts/records.mjs`; its one "experimental" load warning is dropped in that script only.

Every workspace `package.json` is `"private": true` and named `@ezdraft/<name>` (`app`, `geometry`, `worker`, `spike-<dir>` in lowercase). The root scripts `typecheck`, `lint`, `test` and `build` fan out to every workspace package except `spikes/*` with `pnpm -r --if-present --filter "!./spikes/**"`; `lint` runs check C1–C10 first and `test` runs `db:up`, then the repo scripts' `node:test` files, then the pan fixture test alone (EZD-374), then the package tests with `--no-bail`, every time, and fails when any part failed; a failed `db:up` runs neither (Q-0326). EZD-001 extends these files; it does not replace them. The geometry package runs in the browser, in Web Workers and in Node (02 Geometry core; 11 Where each output is produced), so it uses no DOM or React.

### Root config files

EZD-001 (vault/plans/EZD-001.md Step 3). There is no root `tsconfig.json`, so a root `tsc` compiles nothing under `vault/`, `spikes/` or `.claude/worktrees/`; every package sets `types` explicitly because default `@types` discovery walks up into the main checkout from a worktree.

- `tsconfig.base.json`: shared compiler options only (`strict`, `target` ES2022, `module` ESNext, `moduleResolution` Bundler, `isolatedModules`, `skipLibCheck`, `noEmit`, `esModuleInterop`, `resolveJsonModule`); no `include` or `files`. `app/tsconfig.json` and `packages/geometry/tsconfig.json` extend it. The geometry config has `lib: ["ES2023"]` and `types: []`: no DOM, no Node. `packages/geometry/tsconfig.test.json` extends the geometry config with the same `lib` and `types` and type-checks `src/**/*.test.ts` with the sources they import; `packages/geometry/tsconfig.config.json` type-checks `vitest.config.ts` alone (Node types reach it through `vitest/config`, Q-0292 (a)); the geometry `typecheck` script runs the source program, the test program, then the config program, so `pnpm typecheck` covers all three (EZD-161).
- `eslint.config.mjs`: one flat config. Ignores `node_modules`, `.next`, `dist`, `coverage`, Playwright output, `app/next-env.d.ts`, `.claude/**`, `vault/**`, `spikes/**`. All TS/JS gets `@eslint/js` recommended and `typescript-eslint` recommended (not type-checked); `app/**` alone adds the `eslint-config-next` flat configs (core-web-vitals, typescript), and its settings block sets `react.version` to `app/package.json`'s `react` version (`pinnedReactVersion`, `eslint-rules/react-version.mjs`), because `eslint-config-next`'s `detect` resolves `react` from the repo root when a root test lints a path that is not on disk (EZD-186; Q-0324). Each package's `lint` script is `eslint .`.
- `.prettierrc.json` (`singleQuote`, `printWidth` 100) and `.prettierignore` (`vault/`, `spikes/`, `.claude/`, `.github/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/`, every `*.md`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, build outputs, and all of `scripts/` except `scripts/conventions/check.mjs`). Root scripts `format` and `format:check`.
- `vitest.config.mts` (root): `test.projects` = `app/vitest.config.mts` and `packages/*/vitest.config.ts`, so a root `vitest` run reaches only the package projects.
- Vitest configs in a package without `"type": "module"` (root, app) are `.mts`, so Vite loads them as ESM; a `.ts` config there prints the `configLoader: 'native'` warning "ESM syntax in a file loaded as CommonJS" (EZD-179, Q-0314). Never set `VITE_CONFIG_NATIVE_IGNORE_WARNING`.
- `pnpm-workspace.yaml`: `packages`, `saveExact: true`, `workspaceConcurrency: 1` (Q-0139 (a); pnpm 12 ignores `.npmrc` for these) and the `allowBuilds` map (Adding a dependency). Added keys are scalars or maps, never `- ` list lines (EZD-000 T26 reads every `- ` line as a package glob).

Internal packages are TypeScript source with no build step: `packages/geometry/package.json` has `"type": "module"` and `exports["."] = "./src/index.ts"`, and consumers compile it. The first ticket that imports it adds `"@ezdraft/geometry": "workspace:*"` to its `package.json` and, in the app, `transpilePackages: ['@ezdraft/geometry']` in `app/next.config.ts`. `app/next.config.ts` pins `outputFileTracingRoot` and `turbopack.root` to the workspace root because worktrees sit inside the main checkout and Next.js otherwise infers the root from the nearest lockfile upward.


### Design tokens, fonts and icons

EZD-010a (vault/plans/EZD-010a.md Step 4); 04 Frame and visual system (Studio); Q-0026 (a); Q-0040 (c); Q-0057 (b); Q-0013 (a).

- Names use the `--ez-` prefix; radii are named by value.
- `app/src/styles/tokens.css` holds every literal color and shadow, `tokens.ts` (`TOKENS`) mirrors it entry for entry. Values come from 04 Frame and visual system or a cited Studio board (vault/tickets/README.md rule 5). A ticket that adds a token appends it to both files with its board and Q-ID in a comment. Components use `var(--ez-*)` only (check C10).
- Measurements, code and numbers use DM Mono (`var(--ez-font-mono)`, EZD-010c) (04 Type).
- Colors: the spec's `oklch()` values ship as written with no sRGB fallback; browser floor Firefox 113+, Safari/iOS 16.4+, Chrome/Edge 111+ (Q-0026 (a); Q-0013 (a)).
- Board-cited exception radii approved by Josh: `--ez-radius-6` for tooltips (Q-0040 (c), added by EZD-012) and `--ez-radius-14` for dialogs (Q-0057 (b), added by EZD-015). Any other radius that conflicts with 04's list becomes a question.
- `tokens.css` is excluded from Prettier, because Prettier rewrites `rgba(20,30,50,.05)` to `rgba(20, 30, 50, 0.05)` and the values must stay the spec text (`.prettierignore`).
- Icons (EZD-010b; vault/plans/EZD-010b.md): source `github.com/google/material-design-icons`, folder `symbols/web/<name>/materialsymbolsoutlined/`, pinned commit `49d4db35df873165d6bd6ba09b063c7dafbac2f4` (`ICON_COMMIT` in `app/scripts/icons-fetch.mjs`), Apache-2.0.
- Icon style: Outlined, weight 400, grade 0, optical size 20; FILL 0, with `<name>-fill` for FILL 1.
- Icons draw from the in-page sprite (EZD-347; D-088): the root layout renders `IconSprite` first in `<body>` (`<svg display="none">`, one `<symbol id="ez-icon-<name>">` per `ICON_NAMES` entry from `icon-symbols.generated.ts`), and `Icon` uses `<use href="#ez-icon-<name>">`, so an icon first drawn offline makes no request. Only `IconSprite.tsx` imports `icon-symbols.generated.ts` and only `app/src/app/layout.tsx` imports `IconSprite` (`IconSprite.test.tsx` U4).
- To add an icon: run `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app icons:fetch <name>`, add the name to `icons.json` in sorted order, run `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app icons:build`, then commit the SVG, the sprite, the names file and the symbols file. `icons.test.ts` (T8) fails otherwise.
- To check the committed files against `ICON_COMMIT`: `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app icons:verify` (network; never in tests or the gate; EZD-171); a converted icon is checked through its kept original in `__fixtures__/legacy/`, and `CONVERTED.md` is skipped.
- Legacy-format icons (Q-0322): an upstream file without a viewBox is refused by `icons:build`; convert it with `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app icons:convert-legacy <in> <out>` (M/L/H/V/Z only; absolute x × 48, y × 48 − 960; relative deltas × 48), keep the fetched original under `src/components/icon/__fixtures__/legacy/` with a byte-equality test, and list it in `svg/CONVERTED.md` (Apache-2.0 4(b)). `icons:fetch <name>` overwrites the converted file and `icons:build` then exits 1: run the converter again. Converted: `auto_awesome` (EZD-103a).
- `Icon` is decorative, so the button around it carries the label and tooltip (11 Accessibility).
- Never load the icon font, and never write the words "Material Symbols" in `app/src/` or `app/public/` (EZD-010c T7).
- Fonts (EZD-010c; vault/plans/EZD-010c.md): the files in `app/src/styles/fonts/` are copied unchanged from `@fontsource/instrument-sans` and `@fontsource/dm-mono` 5.3.0 (not installed as packages), OFL-1.1, licences next to them. Instrument Sans 400/500/600 for UI; DM Mono 400/500 for measurements, code and numbers.
- Latin subset only (11 rule 8): it covers ASCII, Spanish accents and punctuation, and the measurement marks `'` `"` `×` `°` `…`.
- Loaded with `next/font/local` in `app/src/styles/fonts.ts` as `--ez-font-sans` and `--ez-font-mono`, set on `<html>` by the root layout. Never load Google Fonts (`next/font/google`, `fonts.googleapis.com`, `fonts.gstatic.com`) or the icon font.

### Events

EZD-022 (vault/plans/EZD-022.md Step 4); 07 Hooks, events and integrations.

- The registry is `app/src/lib/events/registry.ts` (`EVENTS`, `EventName`, `EventPayloads`); `emit` is in `app/src/server/events/`. Call `emit` by its bare name through a relative import, never aliased (no app file uses the `@/*` alias yet and `app/vitest.config.mts` sets no alias).
- A new name goes into `EVENTS` in the same commit as its first emitter; screen events not named in 07 are added as `log_only`.
- Payload types are narrowed in `EventPayloads` by the emitting ticket.
- Sinks are added to `defaultSinks` in `app/src/server/events/index.ts` (EZD-109 event log, EZD-1301 webhooks).
- `user.joined` (`log_only`) is emitted by `completeSignIn` when a sign-in accepts an invite (`via: 'sign_in'`, EZD-007d) and later by EZD-105's invite link (`via: 'invite_link'`).
- `room.closure_overridden` (`internal`, `{ roomId, gapIn, userId }`) is emitted by `POST /api/projects/[id]/objects` once per saved room whose closure override is new or changed against its stored row (EZD-205b).

### Messages and language (t())

EZD-026c, EZD-026d, EZD-026a (vault/plans/EZD-026a.md Steps 1-5); R26 R17; R24 #40; 03 Tables user; Q-0028; Q-0030 (a); Q-0163.

- **Files.** `app/src/lib/messages/` (browser and server): `types.ts` (`Language`, `Surface`, `Message`, `MessageParams`), `language.ts` (`languageFor`, `NAMESPACES`, `SPANISH_NAMESPACES`, `KEY_PATTERN`), `t.ts` (`createT`, `T`, `MissingMessageError`, `MissingMessageParamError`), `MessagesProvider.tsx` (`MessagesProvider`, `useT`), `index.ts` re-exporting them. `app/src/server/messages/` (server-only, never in a client bundle): `catalogs/en.ts` (`en`, `MessageKey`), `catalogs/es.ts` (`es`, `SpanishKey`), `catalog.ts` (`catalogFor`), `get-t.ts` (`getT`, for server components). `app/src/app/dev/messages/` holds the test-page components `SampleText` and `MessagesSample`.
- **Surfaces and language.** Surfaces are `office`, `field`, `client`, `dev` and `sign_in`. The office editor and the client link are English in v1; the field pack and the other crew phone screens show the user's language (`app_user.language`, `en` or `es`), never a browser locale or a cookie (R26 R17; 03 user). `languageFor(surface, user)` returns it; its parameter excludes `sign_in`. `sign_in` is the signed-out pages `/sign-in`, `/auth/confirm` and `/terms` (EZD-007f; EZD-1707), whose language is the device's choice from `getSignInLanguage()` (EZD-007d; Q-0047 (a)). Spanish users always see Spanish, never English or blanks (R24 #40), so there is no English fallback on a Spanish surface.
- **Namespaces per surface.** `office`: every namespace except `dev`. `field`: `common`, `field`, `error`. `client`: `common`, `client`, `error`. `dev`: `common`, `field`, `error`, `dev`. `sign_in`: `common`, `error`. `SPANISH_NAMESPACES` (`common`, `field`, `error`, `dev`) need Spanish text for every key; `client` and `office` are English only. `catalogFor('office' | 'client', 'es')` throws; `catalogFor('sign_in', 'es')` is allowed.
- **Key format.** `KEY_PATTERN`: `<namespace>.<lower_snake>[.<lower_snake>...]`, for example `error.db_unavailable`.
- **Text syntax.** `{name}` placeholders (a missing param throws `MissingMessageParamError`); a plural message is `{ one, other }` and needs a numeric `count`, chosen with `Intl.PluralRules` for the language. Numbers are inserted as given, with no locale grouping or decimal comma.
- **Use.** A server layout or page resolves the language, calls `catalogFor(surface, language)` and passes the result to `MessagesProvider` unchanged (no cast of its own); client components call `useT()`. The hook result is always named `t`, because the lint relies on that name. `useT()` and `getT` are typed over every `MessageKey`, but the provider holds only the surface's namespaces, so typecheck does not catch a key from another surface; such a key throws `MissingMessageError` at run time (`app/src/lib/messages/t.ts`), the lint's `keyNamespace` check catches a literal one in the phone and test-page folders, and office components are checked by review.
- **Adding a string.** Add the `en` entry; for a Spanish namespace add the `es` entry in the same commit. The ticket's builder writes the Spanish text in that commit, and a Spanish-speaking crew member checks it at the field checks and in Phase 15 (Q-0030 (a)). Shell and office strings use `office.` keys, English only.
- **Route error keys.** A route that returns a `message_key` adds it to `en`, and to `es` when its namespace needs Spanish, in the same commit. Route error `message_key` values live in the `error` namespace. `message-keys.test.ts` checks every literal `message_key` in `app/src`; a key built at run time is checked by review.
- **Lint.** `ezdraft/no-literal-ui-string` (`eslint-rules/no-literal-ui-string.mjs`, registered by `eslint-rules/index.mjs`; blocks in `eslint.config.mjs`) reports JSX text with a letter, a text expression as a JSX child, a text attribute (`alt`, `title`, `placeholder`, `label`, `aria-label`, names ending in `Label`, `Title`, `Text`, `Message` and similar) and, with `allowedKeyPrefixes`, a `t('<literal>')` key outside the folder's namespaces. Test files are exempt. Folders: field and crew phone `app/src/app/(field)/`, `app/src/components/field/`, `app/src/components/phone/`, `app/src/components/states/` (prefixes `common.`, `field.`, `error.`); client link `app/src/app/(client)/`, `app/src/components/client/` (`common.`, `client.`, `error.`); test page `app/src/app/dev/messages/` (adds `dev.`); sign-in pages `app/src/app/sign-in/`, `app/src/app/auth/confirm/`, `app/src/app/terms/`, `app/src/components/auth/` (`common.`, `error.`; EZD-007e; EZD-1707). `(field)` and `(client)` are App Router route groups and add no URL segment. A ticket that adds a phone folder (EZD-015's states, EZD-020's entry points, EZD-007's sign-in) changes the block's `files` in its own commit. Office components are not linted by the rule.

- **Request language** (EZD-026b, vault/plans/EZD-026b.md Step 3; Q-0029 (a); Q-0087): `getRequestLanguage(surface)` in `app/src/server/messages/request-language.ts` is the only way a page resolves the language. Office and client return `en` with no query. Field and dev read `app_user.language` for `session.appUserId` once per request through `createSessionClient()` (the gated server path), and throw `NotSignedInError` with no session. There is no claim or cookie. A user record always has a language; a new user gets the column default `en`, and the inviter or the user switches to Spanish in settings. The test page is `/dev/messages` (not found in production).

### Shell header (EZD-011)

EZD-011a (vault/plans/EZD-011a.md Step 7); 04 Frame and visual system (Studio); S03 `.card`, `.b`, `.bp`.

- `app/src/components/shell/` holds the shell parts. `card.module.css` is the one card surface (background, border, radius 12, card shadow); other parts compose it with `composes`, never copy it.
- `HeaderCard` (`HeaderCard.tsx`, `HeaderCard.module.css`) is the 60 px header card. It owns its height and its 16 px top and side margin, so the frame adds none. It is server-safe and renders one `<header>`.
- `HeaderButton` (`HeaderButton.tsx`, `HeaderButton.module.css`) is the secondary and primary header button: `variant` is `secondary` (default) or `primary`; every other prop, including `ref`, passes through to the `<button>`.
- `index.ts` re-exports the shell parts.
- The body margin reset lives in `app/src/styles/globals.css`.

EZD-011b (vault/plans/EZD-011b.md Step 6); 04 Header card.

- `EditorHeader` (`EditorHeader.tsx`) is the editor header content inside `HeaderCard`. `EditorHeaderProps` slots render in this order after the view tabs: `reviewPill`, `share`, `export`, `issueSet`. An empty slot renders nothing; slot owners hide their own control with `can()`.
- Board positions reserved for later tickets, each added as its own optional prop: presence avatars before the review pill (S06), Stop editing between the pill and Share (S21), the field-pack chip between the title block and the tabs (S22).
- `EditorView` and `EDITOR_VIEWS` (`editor-views.ts`) are the one view type; EZD-120b imports them.
- Header strings are `office.header.*` keys in `en` only (office is English in v1).
- `EditorHeader` is `memo`, so callers pass stable callbacks (`useCallback`) and no per-frame props.

### Editor shell (EZD-012)

EZD-012a, EZD-012b (vault/plans/EZD-012b.md Step 6); 04 Frame and visual system (Studio), Tool rail, Status bar.

- Files in `app/src/components/shell/`: `EditorFrame` (`EditorFrame.tsx`, `EditorFrame.module.css`, type `EditorFrameProps`), `StatusBar` and `StatusItem` (`StatusBar.tsx`), `EmptyEditor` (`EmptyEditor.tsx`), and EZD-012a's `ToolRail` (`ToolRail.tsx`) with its entry types in `rail-types.ts`. All are client components that read text through `useT()` with `office.` keys (English only).
- `EditorFrame` renders, in order: the header slot, the body (rail, left panel, canvas, inspector), the status bar. Regions carry `data-shell-region`: `frame`, `body`, `rail`, `left-panel`, `canvas`, `inspector`, `status-bar`. Status bar parts carry `data-status` (`start`, `end`), `data-status-item`, `data-status-label`, `data-status-value`. Do not rename them; EZD-012c, EZD-014 and EZD-032 select on them.
- The header slot is unwrapped: `HeaderCard` owns its height and margin, so the frame adds none.
- Sizes are the 04 sizes, border-box, taken from the frame tokens in `tokens.css` (left panel 248, inspector 336, status bar 30, grid 32, body padding and gap 16).
- With `backdrop` the frame drops its CSS grid and the plan canvas draws it (EZD-207a).
- Rail entries are props. EZD-230 owns the tool list, the "More tools" flyout and the per-user order (Q-0038 (b), Q-0122 (a)); a tool appears only when its feature exists. The tooltip is CSS only (EZD-012a).
- Status bar readouts that change per pointer move write through a ref, never React state (11 rule 4). The disclaimer line goes in the status bar (EZD-136, Q-0229).
- The left panel and the inspector `<aside>` render only when their label (`leftPanelLabel`, `inspectorLabel`) is given; an aside always has a label (EZD-120c).
- The shell adds no pointer, scroll or resize listener.

### Typed dimension bar (EZD-209)

- Files: `packages/geometry/src/turn.ts` (`parseTurnAngle`, `resolveTurnDirection`), `app/src/editor/typed-entry/typed-entry.ts` (pure reducer, field consts, `isOpenKey`, `isTypingTarget`), `TypedEntryLayer.tsx` and its CSS module. Not mounted in the real editor; EZD-210 mounts it with the first real tools.
- `TypedEntryLayer` props: `tool`, `canEdit`, `lastValues`, `getCursor` (CSS px from the container's top-left), `onCommit`. It renders nothing when `tool` is null or `canEdit` is false, and goes inside a positioned container (the canvas region).
- Key rule: one capture-phase `keydown` listener on `window` while a tool is active; a key the layer handles gets `stopPropagation()` and `preventDefault()` and does not reach listeners later in the event path (bubble-phase listeners and other targets); it does not stop another capture-phase listener on `window`, so tool shortcut and Esc listeners register in the bubble phase, never capture on `window`.
- Tool shortcuts must skip events whose target passes `isTypingTarget`.
- Test page: `/dev/typed-bar` (`?edit=0` hides the layer; not found in production).

### Drawing tools (EZD-210)

- `app/src/editor/tools/` holds the pure tool modules (no React or DOM), exported from `index.ts`; `DrawingTools.tsx`, `GapBoxes.tsx` and `overlay.ts` beside them are the React and DOM side, never exported from `index.ts` (EZD-210b).
- Reducers take `ToolInput` in model inches after snapping and return the walls to save; they never touch the store.
- Every wall edit goes through `wallEditChanges`, which appends the room reconcile and the opening host updates (EZD-204b).
- The gap box is derived from the walls on every call (`gapBoxes`), never stored.
- `drawing-controller.ts` owns tool state, the snap index and the pointer receiver outside React; `getUi()` changes on tool, pick, gap and last-length changes, never per pointer move (EZD-210b).
- `overlay.ts` writes the SVG band and marker attributes directly on pointer moves and camera changes (EZD-210b).
- V, W, R, Esc, Enter and Ctrl/Cmd+A come from one `window` bubble `keydown` listener in `SelectionLayer`, skipped when `defaultPrevented` or `isTypingTarget`, after EZD-209's capture listener; keys Select does not use go on to the drawing controller (EZD-210b, EZD-208b).
- The rail is `editorRail` (EZD-208a): Select, Wall and Room rectangle, disabled while loading; Select only without Edit model; none when the project failed, is not found or not allowed (EZD-208b).
- Door and Window (EZD-211a): `opening-tool.ts` is a pure reducer like the wall tools but takes `OpeningInput` without snapping (the pointer is projected on the host wall) and returns the openings to save; `G/openings/placement.ts` holds its geometry.
- Door and Window (EZD-211b): `PlanCanvasHandle.onSpaceTap` (no tap after a space-drag); the just-placed or one selected door owns Space, arc, dot and chips (Q-0641, Q-0642); under Select the drawing controller claims arc and dot presses through `SelectionDeps.claim`.

### Selection (EZD-208)

EZD-208a (vault/plans/EZD-208a.md); S03 Canvas interactions; Q-0630; Q-0633; 11 rule 4.

- **Files.** `packages/geometry/src/selection/shapes.ts` holds the pick shapes, exported from the package root; `app/src/editor/selection/pick-index.ts` keeps them on the EZD-206 spatial index; `selection-controller.ts` holds `createSelectionController`, `shareReceiver`, `editorRail` and `SELECT_RAIL_TOOL`; no React.
- **Pick.** Drawn walls and openings on the open floor are the candidates (never rooms): those under the cursor, else those within 10 px, smallest area first, then by id.
- **State.** Session only, never saved; `getUi()` changes only on the ids or Select more; the box goes to `onBox` per move, never through React.
- **Pointer.** `shareReceiver`: the drawing receiver wins over the selection receiver.
- **Screen (EZD-208b).** `app/src/editor/selection/SelectionLayer.tsx`: `SelectionLayer` (the key listener and the box `rect`, written from `onBox`, never through React; inside solid `--ez-cobalt`, touch dashed `--ez-box-touch-stroke`) and `EditBar` (Select more, Select all; shown when `useCoarsePointer()` is true, first in the canvas state column so banners stack under it). `window.__ezdraftSelection` (`ids`, `hoveredId`) with `devHooks`, every viewer.

### Edit commands (EZD-217)

EZD-217 (vault/plans/EZD-217.md Build steps 1-9, section 3); 04 Selection, undo and flags; Q-0630 rows 19-21; Q-0646 (a); Q-0647 (a).

- **Files.** `app/src/editor/edit/`: `edit-changes.ts` (pure: `copyItems`, `clipCenter`, `deleteChanges`, `pasteChanges`) and `edit-controller.ts` (`createEditController`, no React).
- **Rules.** One command = one `applyEdit` = one undo step; delete calls `openingHostUpdates`; paste walls go through `wallEditChanges`; the clipboard is in memory per open project, never the system clipboard.
- **Keys and bar.** Delete/Backspace, Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+C and Ctrl/Cmd+V come through `SelectionLayer`'s one `keydown` listener, edit first; on coarse pointers `EditBar` shows Undo, Redo, Delete, Duplicate, Copy and Paste to editors.

### Status bar popovers (EZD-219)

EZD-219 (vault/plans/EZD-219.md section 1 steps 3, 6, 10, 14, section 3); S03 Controls — status bar; 09m; Q-0664; Q-0680; 11 rule 4.

- **Files.** `app/src/lib/plan/plan-scale.ts` (`PLAN_SCALES`, `PlanScale`, `DEFAULT_PLAN_SCALE` 0.5, `isPlanScale`, `planScaleFromSettings`, `savePlanScale`); `PUT /api/projects/[id]/plan-scale` (`app/src/app/api/projects/[id]/plan-scale/route.ts`, body exactly `{ scale }`, Edit model, `public.set_plan_scale` writes `project.settings.plan_scale`, check `project_settings_plan_scale_check`); `app/src/editor/status/view-settings.ts` (`createSnapSettings`, `deviceSnapSettings`, `createScaleSetting`, labels, `formatCursor`); `app/src/editor/status/PlanStatus.tsx` (`PlanStatusStart`, `PlanStatusEnd`, `PlanScaleFailedBanner`).
- **Snap.** Snap toggles and increment are a device session setting: one `deviceSnapSettings` per tab, reset on reload, never stored; every `snap()` call passes `settings` (`DrawingDeps.snapSettings`).
- **Scale.** The project's plan scale loads in `ModelLoad.project.planScale`; `ScaleSetting` saves an editor's choice one at a time, newest wins, holds it while offline and shows `scale-save-failed-banner` with Retry after a failed save; view-only changes it for the session only.
- **Readouts.** Cursor and Zoom values are spans written through a ref from `onCursorChange` and `onCameraChange`, never React state; a click on Zoom swaps in the typed-zoom field.

### Device layouts (EZD-014)

EZD-014a, EZD-014b, EZD-014c (vault/plans/EZD-014a.md, EZD-014b.md, EZD-014c.md); 02 Platform; 11 Accessibility and input; Q-0053; Q-0054 (a); Q-0055 (a); Q-0056 (a); Q-0088; Q-0098 (a); Q-0245.

- Desktop, Surface and iPad landscape show the full editor; a phone shows the simple layout. Browser tests route by the tags `@ipad`, `@surface` and `@phone` (see `### Browser tests (EZD-016a)`).
- `app/src/lib/device/device-layout.ts` holds `FULL_SHELL_MIN_WIDTH` (1024), `DESKTOP_REFERENCE_WIDTH` (1600), `PHONE_LAYOUT_QUERY`, `COMPACT_HEADER_QUERY` and `COARSE_POINTER_QUERY`. The CSS files that repeat 1024 and 1600 have tests that keep them equal.
- `DeviceLayout` renders one tree at a time with one `change` listener and no resize listener; the server markup is the full shell. `EmptyEditor` takes a `phone` default.
- `app/src/components/phone/` holds three phone components, covered by the lint, importing nothing from `components/shell/` or `components/device/`. EZD-1404, EZD-1013 and EZD-1202 fill them. Pages pass the field catalog (`/dev/device` uses `?lang=es` until EZD-026b).
- 44 px targets: `--ez-touch-target` applies under `@media (any-pointer: coarse)`; `touch.module.css` `.target` and `.hitArea` serve later controls; `:hover` styles sit only under `@media (hover: hover)`, except the rail pressed rule; the rail tooltip also shows on focus (Q-0245). `expectTouchTargets(page, root)` in `app/test-harness/touch-targets.ts` measures them. The Projects page tabs and sort button add an invisible 48 px hit area and its sort menu items are 44 px tall with every pointer (`Projects.module.css`, EZD-330), because EZD-289 E1 runs the check there at desktop size.
- Header: only the title block shrinks. Below 1600 px `HeaderButton` with `icon` shows the icon (Share `share`, Export `download`, EZD-126 `edit`); Issue set keeps its text.
- The test page is `/dev/device` (`?lang=es` shows the phone notice in Spanish; not found in production).
- `useCoarsePointer()` (`app/src/lib/device/useDeviceLayout.ts`, EZD-208b) is `COARSE_POINTER_QUERY` as a hook; true means a tablet (Q-0633).

### State components (EZD-015)

EZD-015a, EZD-015b, EZD-015c (vault/plans/EZD-015a.md, EZD-015b.md, EZD-015c.md); S01 States; 11 Accessibility and input; Q-0030 (a); Q-0057 (b); Q-0058 (a).

- `app/src/components/states/` exports `Skeleton`, `SkeletonGroup`, `AsyncView`, `useAsync`, `InlineError`, `OfflineBanner`, `OfflineBannerView`, `StateHost`, `ToastProvider`, `useToast`, `ConfirmProvider`, `useConfirm`, `TOAST_DEFAULT_MS`; `app/src/lib/network/` exports `createOnlineSource`, `useOnline`.
- S01 States pattern: loading is `AsyncView` with a `SkeletonGroup`; an error is `InlineError` with the screen's own key and Retry; offline is `OfflineBanner` (default `common.offline`); toasts go through `useToast().show`; confirmations go through `useConfirm()`, which answers `'confirm'` or `'cancel'`, and the caller supplies `confirmLabel`.
- `StateHost` is mounted once per entry point, inside `MessagesProvider`.
- `AsyncView`'s `load` is stable (`useCallback`); a new identity starts a new load.
- Offline means `navigator.onLine` is false; read it with `useOnline` and add no `online` or `offline` listener.
- Plain toasts clear after `TOAST_DEFAULT_MS` (5000 ms) or their own duration; action toasts stay until used or closed; one one-shot `setTimeout` per toast.
- The confirm dialog is Radix `AlertDialog` from `radix-ui`, imported only in `ConfirmDialog.tsx`; Esc is Cancel, focus starts on Cancel, and the content carries the provider's `lang`; z-index: overlay 100, dialog 101, toast viewport 102.
- Strings `common.retry`, `common.offline`, `common.cancel` and `common.close` exist in `en` and `es`; buttons are `StateButton` with `touch.target` (44 px on coarse pointers).
