EZD-006 (vault/plans/EZD-006.md Steps 1–5, 8); 03 intro; 03 rule 1; 11 rule 10; 11 Operations; 11 Security and audit; R26 R1; Q-0043 (a); Q-0044 (b); Q-0087 (ii); Q-0052 (c). Seven migrations after EZD-005's: `create_private_schema`, `create_api_request_gate`, `create_rls_helpers`, `create_custom_access_token_hook`, `create_end_sessions_triggers`, `create_tenancy_policies`, `restrict_api_table_grants`.

- **Schema `private`** holds functions only (the audit's `table_in_private_schema` code): the helpers, the token hook and the request gate, all `security definer`, `set search_path = ''`, owned by `postgres`. It is not in `[api] schemas` (`supabase/config.toml` stays `public, graphql_public`), so nothing in it is callable through the REST API. `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role` and `supabase_auth_admin` hold usage only; `pg_default_acl` holds no row for `private` on the pinned stack, so PUBLIC gets execute on a new function: every migration revokes it by name (`revoke all on function private.<fn>(…) from public;`) and grants by name. `anon` can execute exactly one function in it, `api_request_gate`. The `@smoke` audit (`auditRls`) also checks every `private` function and procedure (EZD-168): PUBLIC may execute none (`public_executes_private_function`); `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` may execute only the functions listed for that role in `PRIVATE_EXECUTE` (`app/test-harness/rls/private-execute.ts`; `<role>_executes_private_function`), and every listed function must still be executable by that role (`<role>_private_execute_stale`). A ticket that grants execute on a `private` function to an API role adds its entry with a reason in the same commit, and the security-reviewer approves it.
- **Claims** (the token hook `private.custom_access_token_hook(jsonb)`, executable by `supabase_auth_admin` only; local `config.toml` `[auth.hook.custom_access_token]` `uri = "pg-functions://postgres/private/custom_access_token_hook"`; each cloud project enables it by hand, `docs/runbook.md`). One indexed query per token: `app_user` by `auth_user_id`, left-joined to the `active` memberships. `app_user_id` is set when an `app_user` row is linked to the auth user; `tenant_id`, `membership_id` and `project_scope` (`all` or `listed`) are set when exactly one `active` membership exists. Two or more active memberships set `app_user_id` only, so the person reads no tenant rows (Q-0043 (a): one active company per person; EZD-105 and EZD-007 refuse a second). `invited` and `deactivated` memberships never produce a tenant claim. No row: the claims are returned unchanged; the hook never fails sign-in. EZD-007 links `auth_user_id` at first sign-in and refreshes the session so the hook runs again.
- **Helpers** (executable by `authenticated` only; they read the claims with `auth.jwt()`): `private.session_is_live()` (the token's `session_id` still exists in `auth.sessions`); `private.tenant_id()` and `private.app_user_id()` (the claim, or null; they raise SQLSTATE `42501` `session_ended` when the session is gone, which is how a deactivated user's next request is refused even though the JWT is still valid); `private.all_projects()`; `private.listed_project_ids()` (the membership's `membership_project` rows, read live, one primary-key range scan); `private.tenant_user_ids()`; `private.can_see_project(uuid)` for one-off checks such as EZD-018's channel join.
- **Policy template.** Every policy is `to authenticated`; `anon` gets none; names are `<table>_<command>`. A table with `tenant_id` and `project_id` uses, for every USING and WITH CHECK, `tenant_id = (select private.tenant_id()) and ((select private.all_projects()) or project_id = any ((select private.listed_project_ids())::uuid[]))`, optionally `and` more conditions; a table with `tenant_id` only uses the first clause. Always wrap a helper in a scalar subquery, `(select private.tenant_id())`, so PostgreSQL runs it once per statement as an InitPlan (11 rule 10: the tenant comes from the claim, never from a membership join per row; the audit's `helper_not_wrapped` and `policy_joins_membership` codes). The `::uuid[]` cast is required: PostgreSQL parses `= any ((select f()))` as a row subquery and fails with `uuid = uuid[]`. RLS checks tenant and project scope only, never permission rows (EZD-102). Tenancy policies: `tenant` `id = (select private.tenant_id())`; `app_user` own row or any user of the claim tenant (`private.tenant_user_ids()`); `membership` by `tenant_id`; `membership_project` and `project` by tenant and scope; `project` also has insert, update and delete policies. Every public table `authenticated` can reach also has the restrictive policy `<table>_api_gate` (`as restrictive for all to authenticated using ((select private.require_api_gate())) with check ((select private.require_api_gate()))`), and every `security definer` function in `public` that `anon` or `authenticated` can execute (except `health`) calls `private.require_api_gate()` first, so a request PostgREST served without its pre-request hook still reads and changes nothing (EZD-190; Q-0330).
- **Session ending** (`private.end_user_sessions(uuid)`, no API role may execute it; EZD-018 redefines it with `create or replace`, same signature, adding the `session.ended` push, Q-0052 (c)): trigger `membership_end_sessions` (after update or delete) ends the person's sessions when `status` or `scope` changes or the row is deleted, except a status change from `invited` to `active` (invite acceptance at sign-in; EZD-007b, Q-0044 (b)); a change of `user_id` ends the sessions of both the old and the new person, and a change of `tenant_id` ends the person's sessions (EZD-165); trigger `membership_project_end_sessions` ends them when a listed project is deleted or its `membership_id` / `project_id` changes. Inserting a listed project does not end sessions, because listed projects are read live (Q-0044 (b)). Sessions end in every tenant and on every device. EZD-129 writes these rows as inserts and deletes and never bypasses the triggers. A `tenant_id` change on a membership is refused with 23503 by the foreign keys from `membership_project`, `user_permission` and `membership.role_id` (each `(…, tenant_id)` with no `on update`) while the membership has listed projects, permission rows or a role, so the `tenant_id` branch runs only for a membership with none of them (EZD-165).
- **Tenant-less tables** (`TENANTLESS_TABLES` in `app/test-harness/rls/tenancy-exemptions.ts`, same list as `### Tenancy tables (EZD-005)`): `tenant` (it is the tenant), `app_user` (one person across tenants, linked by `membership`), `sign_in_throttle` (EZD-033: rows are written before sign-in and belong to no company; they hold only keyed hashes and times, Q-0094), `backup_run` (EZD-025: system-wide off-site backup and restore-drill records; one run covers every tenant; no tenant data; no API role can read it) and `error_tunnel_throttle` (EZD-143: the per-address count of error-tunnel events; rows are written for anonymous error reports and belong to no company; keyed hashes and times only; no API role can read it). They still have RLS and a policy. A later exemption adds its reason to both places and needs the security-reviewer's approval.
- **Grants.** The four tenancy tables `tenant`, `app_user`, `membership`, `membership_project` are read-only to `authenticated` (`select` only; `TABLE_GRANTS` in `app/test-harness/rls/table-grants.ts` records the reason per table): a signed-in member must not change their own status, scope or memberships; EZD-105, 106, 112 and 129 add reviewed write paths. `project` grants `select, insert, update, delete` to `authenticated`. `service_role` gets all four on every table (it bypasses RLS but still needs table privileges). `anon` holds no table privilege; `public.health()` keeps its explicit `anon` execute grant. TRUNCATE, REFERENCES and TRIGGER are never granted to an API role.
- **Table grants convention.** Every migration that creates a table or view in `public` grants explicitly, by table name: `select, insert, update, delete` to `authenticated` (or its `TABLE_GRANTS` set, with a reason) and to `service_role`, nothing to `anon`, `usage` on its sequences. `grant … on all tables in schema public` does not count. Default privileges in `public` grant nothing: `restrict_api_table_grants` revoked `postgres`'s default table privileges for `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` (the local CLI, and the cloud setting "Automatically expose new tables", work through `pg_default_acl` for role `postgres` in `public`, confirmed on CLI 2.121.0 / Postgres 17.11; migrations run as `postgres`). So the app does not depend on that setting (left on; matches the local CLI). Every such migration also enables RLS itself; the cloud setting "Enable automatic RLS" is left off, so local and cloud match. The `@smoke` audit enforces all of it (`grants_not_explicit`, `authenticated_grants_mismatch`, `service_role_grants_missing`, `rls_disabled`).
- **Function grants convention.** A migration that creates a `public` function revokes execute from `public`, `anon` and `service_role` by name (`revoke all on function public.<fn>(…) from public, anon, service_role;`) and grants only what the function needs (`grant execute … to authenticated;`), because default privileges for role `postgres` in `public` grant execute on a new function to `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` (confirmed on CLI 2.121.0 / PostgreSQL 17.11; EZD-007b finding B3). A function callable by `anon` or `authenticated` goes into `GATED_RPC_CALLS` (`app/test-harness/rls/gate.ts`), or T22 (j) fails. First applied by EZD-007b (`link_app_user`, `current_app_user_id`). A test-only function (`test` or `dev` as a whole word of its name) is never executable by `anon` or `authenticated` and is listed in `TEST_ONLY_FUNCTIONS` with a reason; the `@smoke` audit enforces it (EZD-323).
- **API request gate** (Q-0087 (ii)). `private.api_request_gate()` is registered as `pgrst.db_pre_request` on role `authenticator` (`alter role authenticator set …; notify pgrst, 'reload config'`; PostgREST v16.4 on the pinned CLI), so PostgREST runs it before every request: tables, RPC and GraphQL (`/graphql/v1` goes through PostgREST). It reads the request role from `current_setting('role')` (not `current_user`, which is the owner inside a definer function): `service_role` passes; `anon` passes only for `request.path` `/rpc/health`; every other `anon` or `authenticated` request must carry the header `x-ezd-api-gate` whose SHA-256 hex digest equals the stored one, else SQLSTATE `42501`. The digest is stored in Supabase Vault (supabase_vault 0.3.1: `vault.create_secret`, `vault.update_secret`, `vault.decrypted_secrets`) under `ezd_api_gate_sha256` by `private.set_api_gate_hash(text)` (no API role may execute it; it accepts only 64 lower-case hex characters). No stored digest: fails closed. Auth, Storage and Realtime do not go through PostgREST and are not behind it (EZD-132 keeps Storage policies tight). Locally `pnpm db:up` stores a fresh hash and writes the secret to the git-ignored `supabase/.temp/ezd-api-gate-secret` (lane n: `.lanes/lane<n>/supabase/.temp/ezd-api-gate-secret`, EZD-172); the Vitest global setup stores its own per-run secret; `node scripts/db/api-gate.mjs generate` prints a secret and the SQL line for Josh's cloud step (`docs/runbook.md`). A ticket that adds a `public` function callable by `anon` or `authenticated` adds it to `GATED_RPC_CALLS` (`app/test-harness/rls/gate.ts`), or T22 (j) fails.

### Permissions and roles (EZD-101)

EZD-101 (vault/plans/EZD-101.md Steps 1-10); 03c user_permission, 03d role; 10; S15 Permission list; Q-0034; Q-0210; Q-0223; Q-0224; Q-0225; Q-0234; Q-0235.

- **Keys.** 37 switches in 6 groups (`projects` 7, `renders` 5, `money` 5, `issue_send` 4, `field` 6, `admin` 10), listed in order in `private.permission_keys()` and in `PERMISSIONS` (`app/src/lib/permissions/catalog.ts`; `PermissionKey`, `isPermissionKey`). The same literal is repeated in the check constraints of `role.default_permissions` and `user_permission.permission_key`, because a check calling a private function fails as `authenticated`. Q-0234 removed Approve translations and the Manage users and settings switch became the page switches `settings_*`, ending with `settings_system_status` (Q-0210, Q-0225). "Own jobs" is `membership.scope = 'listed'`, not a key (Q-0202).
- **Presets** (`private.preset_permissions`): Owner 37, Designer 26, Foreman 11, Crew 4 (Q-0223, Q-0224, Q-0235); any other name raises `22023` `unknown_preset`.
- **`public.role`** (tenant-owned): `preset` null is a custom role with `default_permissions` (a set of keys). Trigger `tenant_seed_preset_roles` gives every new tenant the four preset rows through `private.seed_preset_roles` (idempotent). `role_preset_guard` (`private.role_preset_guard`, before update or delete) refuses any change to a preset row, or setting `preset` on a custom one, with `preset_role_change_refused` (`P0001`) for every caller; a delete that cascades from a tenant delete passes. Members may insert (preset null only), update and delete custom roles; a role held by a membership cannot be deleted (foreign key, no action).
- **`public.user_permission`** (tenant-owned): one row per switch per membership, primary key `(membership_id, permission_key)`. Read-only to `authenticated` (a member must not raise their own switches); EZD-106 adds the reviewed write path. Triggers `membership_default_role` (before insert) sets `membership.role_id` from `app_user.role_preset` and `membership_seed_permissions` (after insert) writes the 37 rows through `private.seed_member_permissions`, which never overwrites. Later role changes re-tick nothing; EZD-106's Save does.
- **EZD-102** reads a member's set with `select permission_key from user_permission where membership_id = <claim membership_id> and granted`.

### Release safety (EZD-023a)

EZD-023a (vault/plans/EZD-023a.md Steps 1–5); 11 Operations ("add before they remove"); R26 R12; Q-0106; Q-0112; Q-0113. The check is `scripts/release/check-migrations.mjs`, run by the root `lint` script, so every gate run and CI run it (no ci.yml edit, `### CI`). EZD-023b's release workflow runs it again on the merge commit with `--base <previous tag> --head <sha>` before it changes production. `--base` must be a release tag and `--head` a release tag or a lower-case commit sha (7 to 40 hex characters); both are checked before any git process starts, else the line `invalid_ref` and exit 1 (Q-0156 (a)).

- **Release tags.** `phase-<N>` is the release at the end of phase N; `phase-<N>.<k>` (k ≥ 1) a release Josh approves between phase gates (regex `RELEASE_TAG_RE` in `scripts/release/tags.mjs`; order by N then k; `phase-01` and `phase-1.0` are not release tags). Tags are created only by EZD-023b's release workflow on GitHub, on `main`, after the migrations reach production; nobody makes one locally. The `phase-*` tag ruleset blocks updates and deletes (Q-0112), so `origin`'s tags are the record of what production received. "Previous tag" is the highest release tag that exists; there is no ancestry check (tags sit on `main`'s merge commits, not on `develop`).
- **Tag fetch.** Every run first lists origin's tags with `git ls-remote --tags origin 'refs/tags/phase-*'`; none listed is a successful fetch (a depth-1 fetch of a pattern that matches nothing exits 1 in a shallow clone, Q-0240). Otherwise it does `git fetch --no-tags origin "+refs/tags/phase-*:refs/tags/phase-*"` (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, 30 s timeout, `--depth=1` only in a shallow clone; it writes only `refs/tags/phase-*` and never pushes). In GitHub Actions (`GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`) a failed fetch is **M0** and `--no-fetch` is ignored (fails closed). Locally a failed fetch prints `tags_not_fetched` and the check goes on with local tags (less strict, never stricter); `--no-fetch` prints `tags_not_fetched (--no-fetch)` and is for tests only. A reachable `origin` with no `phase-*` tag is a successful fetch.
- **No tag yet.** Until the first real release (Q-0113) the check prints `no release tag yet: nothing is released` and checks only M5, so pre-launch migrations may drop and rename freely.
- **"Still reads".** An object is read by the previous release when it exists at that tag and is not listed in that tag's `supabase/retired.json`. Removal takes two releases: release N stops reading the object and lists it in `supabase/retired.json` (the code-reviewer checks that no code at that commit reads it); release N+1 may drop or rename it. An object created after the previous tag may be dropped or renamed freely; created means absent at the tag, by kind and name (Q-0156 (a)): the check builds the base object set from the tag's migrations (`findDefined`), and re-declaring a released object (`create or replace …`, `create … if not exists …`, `add column if not exists …`) never makes it new, so dropping it afterwards is still M1. A retired table, view or materialized view covers its columns. Format: `{ "retired": [ { "kind", "name", "ticket", "reason" } ] }`; `kind` is `table`, `view`, `materialized_view`, `function`, `procedure`, `type`, `domain`, `schema`, `sequence`, `extension` or `column`; `name` is lower case, unquoted and schema-qualified (`column` 3 segments, `schema` 1, every other kind 2; no argument list on functions). The file sits outside `supabase/migrations/`, so it triggers neither `supabase-dev.yml` nor check C5.
- **Violations** (exit 1; one line per violation `[Mn] <file>: <object> <detail>`, then a summary naming the base tag): **M0** release tags could not be fetched in GitHub Actions; **M1** a new migration drops or renames an object the base tag still reads (not created by a new migration of a version ≤ its own, not retired at the base tag); **M2** a released migration is changed (compared after normalizing line endings to LF) or removed: never edit a released migration, add a new one; **M3** a new migration's 14-digit version is not newer than the newest released one (`## Migrations` file names); **M4** the SQL cannot be checked statically: a drop ending in `cascade`, or drop or rename text inside a string literal or dollar-quoted body (`execute '…'`, a function body: `drop <kind|column|attribute>`, `rename to|column|value|attribute`, `rename <identifier> to`, `set schema`; stricter, never weaker, so such a body is rewritten or reviewed); **M5** the head's `supabase/retired.json` is missing, not JSON or has a wrong shape, kind or segment count (checked even with no tag).
- **What the scan sees.** Destructive: `drop` of a listed kind (any number of names); `alter table … drop [column]`, `rename [column] … to`, `rename to`, `set schema`; `alter <kind> … rename to | set schema`; `alter view | materialized view … rename [column] … to` (the view's column); `alter type … rename value | rename attribute | drop attribute` (the type). Created: `create [or replace] <kind>` and `alter table … add [column]`. Unquoted names are lower-cased, a missing schema is `public`, `if [not] exists` and `only` are skipped. **Not checked** (the app does not read them, or they are outside "drops or renames"): `drop index`, `drop trigger`, `drop policy`, `drop constraint`, `alter index … rename`, `rename constraint`, `alter column … drop default | not null`, `truncate`, `alter column … type`, `revoke`, and view or function changes that alter a result shape; the code-reviewer watches for those. A widened check constraint (drop, then add) passes (`### Tenancy tables (EZD-005)`).
- Agents cannot run the script by hand (`.claude/hooks/guard-bash.mjs` denies `node scripts/release/…`); it runs through the gate's `lint` and through `node --test` on `scripts/release/__tests__/`. The tests use a bare local `origin` over `file://` and never reach the network (`git-fixture.mjs`, which EZD-023b reuses).
- **Scratch database (EZD-153; Q-0334 (b)).** It runs when there is a base tag, at least one new migration and no M3. The check builds a scratch Supabase stack (`scripts/release/scratch/supabase/config.toml`, project `ezdraft-migcheck`, ports 26320-26329) from the base tag's migrations and stops every service but Postgres; it then applies the new migrations one at a time with `supabase migration up --local --output-format json` and reads the catalog (`SNAPSHOT_SQL`, `scripts/release/scratch-check.mjs`) before and after each. M1 = a base object dropped or renamed (by object id, so a drop and re-create counts) unless retired; M4 = a created object whose create statement the scan does not find in that file, or a migration that fails there. One run per machine (`ezd-migcheck.lock` in the shared `supabase/.temp`); its children get only `SCRATCH_ENV_NAMES`. Before that line the notes give the reason (EZD-259): for a failed pre-start stop, start, `docker ps` or service `docker stop`, a line `scratch <step>: <summary>`, then the CLI's JSON error lines (`scratch <step> error: …`) and its stderr lines without the rerun hint and update notice (`scratch <step> stderr: …`), at most 20 of each, URL passwords redacted, 500 characters a line; a missing db container prints the containers that are running. A failure prints `scratch_db_failed: <code>` and fails the check. `--no-db` is for tests only (the root `lint` and the release never pass it). The scratch holds no rows.

## Errors and logging

11 Operations, Security and audit; R26 R12, R16, R17; EZD-015, EZD-024, EZD-026, EZD-120b.

- Route error body: `{ "error": { "code": "<stable_snake_code>", "message_key": "<t() key>" } }`. Status: 400 invalid input (Zod failure), 401 not signed in, 403 permission missing (EZD-102), 404 row not found or outside the user's tenant or scope (the response does not reveal that it exists), 409 stale `lock_epoch` or sequence conflict (03 rules 6, 9), 500 unexpected. The browser shows `message_key` through `t()` (R26 R17); no raw server text reaches the UI.
- Browser: a failed load uses the inline error with Retry (EZD-015); a failed save keeps the edits and shows the save banner (EZD-120b). Nothing fails silently: anything skipped is counted and shown (R26 R16 pattern).
- Jobs: a failure writes `job.error` and `attempts` (03 job table); retry with backoff (EZD-009); a final failure goes to Sentry and the owner email (11 Operations; EZD-024).
- Logging: one structured JSON line per event from one logger module (`logger.ts`) on the server and in the worker: level, message, route or job kind, tenant_id, project_id, request or job id, duration. Never logged: tokens (share-link, magic-link, 6-digit code, EZ Binder sign-on code), keys and secrets, request bodies, email addresses, money values (03 rules 2, 11; CLAUDE.md rule 8). No `console.*` outside `logger.ts` and tests (check C8). Unexpected errors go to Sentry (EZD-024).

### Error reporting (EZD-024)

EZD-024 (vault/plans/EZD-024.md Steps 1-12); 11 Operations; R26 R12; Q-0067 (b); Q-0068 (a); Q-0069 (a); Q-0070 (a); Q-0035.

- **Flow.** Every error ends in `app/src/server/errors/`. Server errors arrive through `onRequestError` in `app/src/instrumentation.ts` and explicit `reportError()` calls (job runner, the `after()` kick, email failures). Browser errors are sent by the Sentry browser SDK (`app/src/instrumentation-client.ts`) to the app's own tunnel `POST /api/monitoring`, never straight to Sentry. The module scrubs the event, hands it to Sentry through the server client, and records its fingerprint in `error_alert` through `record_error_alert`; only when that hands out a claim does it email the alert with `sendEmail`.
- **`SENTRY_TRANSPORT`:** unset or empty is off (`sentry_off`; reporting never breaks the app, and the owner email does not depend on Sentry); `stub` uses the stub transport (`app/src/server/errors/test-harness/sentry-stub-transport.ts`, file sink `SENTRY_STUB_DIR`), refused in production; `sentry` needs a valid `SENTRY_DSN` and is refused under Vitest (`sentry_live_blocked_in_tests`). No tracing, profiling, replay or default integrations. Runtime channel injection is off (enableRuntimeChannelInjection: false); the stub module loads by import() on its first send; register() and onRequestError load their modules by import() only when NEXT_RUNTIME is not edge (EZD-036). `register()` starts the server client and tags events `ezd.source=function`.
- **Placeholder DSN and tunnel.** The browser always uses `https://public@ezdraft.invalid/1` (`PLACEHOLDER_DSN`, `app/src/lib/errors/browser-options.ts`) with `tunnel: '/api/monitoring'`, so no real DSN or build-time value reaches the browser. The tunnel is public, refuses a body over 262,144 bytes (413 `envelope_too_large`) and an envelope that does not parse or whose header `dsn` is not the placeholder (400 `invalid_envelope`), keeps `event` items only, scrubs them again, forwards them with the server's DSN in `after()` and records each one. The browser SDK packages are `@sentry/browser` (integrations global handlers, linked errors, dedupe); the server uses `@sentry/nextjs`, with `@sentry/core`'s envelope helpers in the tunnel. The browser client starts through `initAndBind` (`app/src/lib/errors/browser-client.ts`), not `Sentry.init`, so the default integrations are not in any entry's download (EZD-341). The tunnel copies the envelope header `event_id` only when it matches `EVENT_ID` (`app/src/lib/errors/scrub.ts`, 32 lower-case hex); otherwise the forwarded header has no `event_id` and the events are still forwarded (EZD-183).
- **Per-address limit (EZD-143).** The tunnel counts event items per client address (`clientIp`, `throttleKey('ip', …)` of EZD-033) in `error_tunnel_throttle` through `public.error_tunnel_take` (anon only, gated): over 60 events in a rolling hour (Q-0276 (a)) the whole envelope gets 429 `too_many_requests` and nothing is forwarded or recorded. Worker-tagged events count too (the tag is the sender's; Q-0277 (a)). No address means no count; a failed count lets the envelope through.
- **Scrub rule** (`app/src/lib/errors/scrub.ts`): URL query and hash dropped; email addresses, UUIDs, tokens (24+ letters, digits, `_` or `-` with a letter and a digit) and 6-digit codes replaced; `request` keeps only `url` and the `User-Agent` header; `user` keeps only `id`; `extra` and `breadcrumbs` removed. `tags`: keys and string values scrubbed, number and boolean values kept, other values removed; an `ezd.job_id` that is exactly one UUID is kept (EZD-040). `contexts`: keys and strings scrubbed at every depth, so a trace id becomes `<token>` (no tracing runs). Stack frames keep only `filename`, `abs_path`, `function`, `raw_function`, `module`, `package`, `context_line`, `pre_context`, `post_context` (scrubbed) and `lineno`, `colno`, `in_app`, so `vars` is removed (EZD-040). The tunnel takes `tags` from the scrubbed event. Owner emails carry only scrubbed titles, ids and codes. Every other event field (EZD-142): `scrubEvent` keeps only the fields listed in vault/plans/EZD-142.md D8-D11 and drops the rest (`server_name`, `modules`, `debug_meta`, `spans`, `measurements`, `sdkProcessingMetadata`, `transaction_info`, unknown keys); thread frames follow the stack frame rule; `logentry`, `sdk` and exception `mechanism` are scrubbed at every depth; `fingerprint` strings and exception `type` and `module` are scrubbed; `release` and `environment` are kept as set; `event_id` only as 32 lower-case hex. `scrubText` runs in linear time: the URL and email passes are scanners; the UUID, token and code patterns are bounded. Owner email titles scrub the error type too.
- **Fingerprint rule** (`app/src/server/errors/fingerprint.ts`): SHA-256 of source, error name, normalized message (scrubbed, hex runs and numbers replaced) and location (browser URL path with ids replaced, the route pattern, the job kind, or `worker`); no stack frames. A failed job is `job_failed`, tenant and job id; a failed webhook delivery `webhook_failed`, tenant and event id (one email each, Q-0068 (a)).
- **`error_alert`** (nullable `tenant_id`, null only for kind `error`, so error rows are visible to no member; a row with a project always has a company (check `error_alert_project_needs_tenant`, EZD-144: the composite foreign key is MATCH SIMPLE and checks nothing when `tenant_id` is null); members read their company's failure rows) is written only by `public.record_error_alert` and `public.release_error_alert_email` (`security definer`, executable by `anon`, `authenticated`, `service_role`, both in `GATED_RPC_CALLS`). A caller other than `service_role` may record only kind `error` from `browser`, `function` or `worker`, with tenant and project forced to null, and at most 100 new error rows per hour from the tunnel (`browser`, `worker`) and, counted apart, 100 from `function` (EZD-143), each group counted under its own transaction lock (EZD-321). The email cap is 20 alerts per rolling hour for all companies (Q-0068 (a)); `browser` and `worker` alerts may use at most 10 of them, so `function` errors, failed jobs and failed deliveries always have room (Q-0278 (b); EZD-143). A failed send releases the claim so a later occurrence can email. The request path reaches the store as `anon` through `createSessionClient` with an empty cookie jar (`alertStoreClient()`), never with the secret key.
- **Who gets alerts** (Q-0067 (b)): new errors go to `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` only; failed jobs and webhooks go to `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` plus the company's Owners (the Owner preset, `app_user.role_preset = 'owner'`, Q-0067 (b)), one message each, duplicate addresses merged; wording `office.alert.*` (English, Q-0069 (a)).
- **Copy link.** System alerts have no sender, so the copy-link rule of Q-0035 does not apply to them; Sentry (and `SENTRY_ISSUES_URL`) and the System status page (EZD-135) are the non-email path.
- **Convention:** a `catch` that turns an unexpected error into a 500 calls `reportError`. Handler throws in the job runner go to Sentry with `alert: false` (`onHandlerError`); the owner email for a job comes only from its final failure (`onJobFailed`, `defaultJobHooks()` in `app/src/server/jobs/default-hooks.ts`, used by the kick and the sweep). Email send failures go to Sentry with `ezd.location` `email.send` and the code only.
- **Route 500s** (EZD-169; Q-0268): a route catch that answers 500 calls `await reportRouteFailure(error, '<route pattern>')` (`app/src/server/errors/route-failure.ts`) before it answers. It reports `new Error('db_unavailable')` with `ezd.location` = the route pattern (`/api/jobs/[id]`) and `ezd.cause` = the caught error's `name`, never its message. `app/src/app/api/route-failure.test.ts` (T7) fails a route file that answers 500 and calls neither it nor `reportError(` (EZD-102's two permission routes call `reportError` directly). `/api/health` is the one exception (EZD-169; Q-0317 (a); Q-0285 (a)): it registers the call with `after()` from `next/server`, so a hung database, which also holds the alert store, cannot delay its 500 past `HEALTH_DB_TIMEOUT_MS`; outside a request scope it starts the call without awaiting it.
- **Webhooks:** EZD-1301 calls `reportWebhookFailure({ db, tenantId, projectId, eventName, eventId, attempts, lastStatus })` with the runner's client when a delivery has used up its retries; `eventName` must be a registered `webhook` event, else it throws `TypeError`.
- **Render worker contract** (EZD-1104b): the worker initialises the Node Sentry SDK at the same `@sentry/*` version with `dsn` = the placeholder DSN, `tunnel` = `<app origin>/api/monitoring`, the tag `ezd.source=worker` and the same `scrubEvent`, so it holds no Sentry secret and no new key, and its errors get the same dedupe and owner email.
- **Live check** `scripts/errors/live-check.mjs`, run by Josh by hand after the first release, refused under `CI`, never by the gate.
- Agents never sign in to Sentry; account steps are in `docs/runbook.md` `## Error reporting (EZD-024)`.

## Storage buckets and paths

03 rules 1, 11; 03 source_file, render, catalog_item, job; 11 rule 12, Operations, Data retention; EZD-025, EZD-132. Rules from the sources: every bucket is private; files are served through short-lived signed links; browser uploads go straight to Storage with resumable signed uploads; job inputs and outputs are Storage paths; originals are never changed; renders are stored full size plus 400 px and 1600 px copies; deletes of sent-package files are soft deletes. Layout (EZD-132, vault/plans/EZD-132.md Steps 1–2 and Decided rows, created the buckets and the first policies):

- Every path starts `<tenant_id>/` so Storage policies use the same tenant claim as table RLS (EZD-006). Project files continue `<project_id>/`; tenant-level files use `<tenant_id>/tenant/`.
- Buckets: `sources` (uploaded originals, every `source_file` kind; written once, never overwritten), `renders` (`<tenant_id>/<project_id>/<render_id>/full|1600|400.<ext>`), `exports` (PDF sets, DXF, GLB, USDZ, CSV, selections PDF, markup PDF/DXF; sent-package files soft-deleted), `media` (plan thumbnails and version previews, logo, catalog images 256 px, spec sheets, textures, camera-view thumbs), `jobs` (`<tenant_id>/<project_id or tenant>/<job_id>/in|out/...` scratch).
- Object names use ids, not user file names; the original file name is kept on the row (03 source_file).
- Job payloads and results name files as `{ bucket, path }` (`storagePathSchema`, `app/src/server/jobs/storage-path.ts`): one of the five buckets; `path` has no leading `/`, no `..` segment and no `\`. The runner checks every such object in a payload and a result: the path starts with `<job tenant_id>/`, and for a project job with `<tenant_id>/<project_id>/` or `<tenant_id>/tenant/` (EZD-009).
- **Buckets** (EZD-132 Step 2, `<ts>_create_storage_buckets.sql`): the five above, `public = false`; no bucket size or type limit except `media` and `sources` (the global 50 MiB of `supabase/config.toml` applies; each upload ticket checks its own sizes; 03 rule 11). `media` (EZD-290, `<ts>_set_media_bucket_limits.sql`) takes only `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/svg+xml` and (EZD-224, plan thumbnails) `image/webp` up to 4,000,000 bytes (Q-0512 (a); the logo's `LOGO_CONTENT_TYPES`, `LOGO_MAX_BYTES`); a later ticket that writes other `media` files widens these limits. `sources` (EZD-296, `<ts>_set_sources_bucket_limits.sql`) takes only `image/vnd.dxf`, `model/gltf-binary`, `application/pdf`, `image/jpeg`, `image/png`, `image/heic` up to 52,428,800 bytes (`SOURCE_CONTENT_TYPES`, `SOURCE_MAX_BYTES`, `app/src/lib/storage/source-file-kinds.ts`; Q-0477); `startSourceFileUpload` refuses any other type or size with `invalid_input`; no wildcard types (R-052: `image/*` admits `image/svg+xml`). `STORAGE_BUCKETS` (`app/src/lib/storage/buckets.ts`) holds the same list as `storagePathSchema`.
- **Logo writes are server-only** (EZD-295, `<ts>_logo_writes_server_only.sql`): `storage_media_insert` also requires `private.storage_request_from_server()` (the request carries the API gate header, which only the server adds) and `storage.operation = 'storage.object.upload'` (no signed, copy, TUS or S3 upload); `storage_media_delete` also requires `private.storage_request_from_server()`.
- **Plan thumbnails** (EZD-224, `<ts>_create_checkpoint_thumbnail.sql`; Q-0664): `media` also takes `image/webp`. Thumbnails live at `<tenant_id>/<project_id>/thumb/<model_version_id>.webp`. `storage_media_thumb_insert` lets an object in only through an upload URL the server signs (`private.storage_request_from_server()`, operation `storage.object.sign_upload_url`) for the signer's own newest checkpoint of a project they may edit (`private.thumbnail_upload_allowed`); there is no update or delete policy for thumbs.
- **Policies** on `storage.objects` (EZD-132 Step 2): only `storage_sources_select`, `storage_sources_insert`, `storage_media_select`, `storage_media_insert`, `storage_media_delete` and (EZD-224) `storage_media_thumb_insert`, all `to authenticated`; no `update` policy (no overwrite, upsert or move), none on `renders`, `exports` or `jobs` (handlers write with the secret key; owner tickets add theirs), none `to anon`. `media` select is scope-only: tenant-level files (`<tenant_id>/tenant/...`) are read by every member of the company, project files by project scope. `media` insert and delete cover only the company logo, `<tenant_id>/tenant/logo/<file>` (exactly three folder segments, EZD-112's path), and only for a member whose own `settings_company` switch is granted (`K2`, read live per statement like `K` below; the Company settings page owns the logo); delete also only by the uploader (`owner_id = auth.uid()`). Every other `media` prefix (project media, catalog images, spec sheets, textures, camera-view thumbs) has no insert or delete policy yet; plan thumbnails: the Plan thumbnails bullet above. A later ticket that writes another `media` prefix from the browser adds an insert (and delete) policy for that prefix carrying the switch its screen checks; a prefix written only by job handlers needs none. Why: Storage is outside the PostgREST gate and the browser holds the access token (EZD-018), so a policy is the only check on a direct Storage call; 03 rule 1's exception covers only a signed upload from a route that ran `can()`.
- **`sources` write gate** (AC-EZD-132-5): an object enters `sources` only where a visible `source_file` row with that `storage_path` has `uploaded_at` null and `uploaded_by` = the signer's own `app_user` (`(select private.app_user_id())`, the same match as `source_file_insert`), so the row comes first, only the member who started the upload can put bytes under it (pending rows stay forever, so without the match any scoped member could fill another member's row), and an uploaded original is never replaced; a row with `uploaded_by` null takes no object. `startSourceFileUpload` (`app/src/server/storage/source-files.ts`) inserts the row, then signs the upload, and `completeSourceFileUpload` checks the object's size and stamps `uploaded_at` once (trigger `source_file_keep_original`, `P0001` `source_file_original_refused`, also refuses any change to the identifying columns). `authenticated` has `select, insert, update` on `source_file`, never `delete` (03c: originals never changed; 11 Data retention). A pending row (`uploaded_at` null) is updated only by its starter (`source_file_update`); `uploaded_by` changes only to null when its `app_user` row is gone (`on delete set null`); stamping `uploaded_at` needs the `sources` object with the row's path and size (EZD-320).
- **Cost files** (Q-0269 (b)): `sources` objects whose `source_file.kind` is `supplier_pdf` or `order_report` are read (download, signed link, info, list) and written (signed upload) only by a member whose own `user_permission` row `see_company_costs` is granted (`membership_id` from the token claim, read live per statement through `K`, so a switch change applies without a new sign-in); any other member gets Storage's normal not-found or RLS refusal. The `source_file` row itself stays scope-only (no money fields, 03 rule 2). `K` and `K2` are the Storage policies that read a permission row, because a Storage policy is the only check on a direct Storage call; the never-permission-rows rule of `### Row-level security (EZD-006)` holds for tables. `startSourceFileUpload` refuses a `supplier_pdf` or `order_report` start, or any start with a supplier name or contact email, from a member without `see_company_costs` with `permission_denied` before any row is written (routes answer 403 `error.not_authorized`); it reads the switch with `loadPermissionContext` on the caller's client, one extra `current_permissions` call for these starts only, so every route that reuses it inherits the check (EZD-322).
- **Paths:** `sources` objects are `<tenant_id>/<project_id>/<source_file_id>` (no extension; `sourceFilePath()`), checked by the table; `media` objects are `<tenant_id>/tenant/...` or `<tenant_id>/<project_id>/...`; the user's file name lives only in `source_file.original_name`.
- **Lifetimes** (Q-0232; R-017 #5): download links through `signedDownloadUrl(client, target, { expiresIn? })` live `SIGNED_DOWNLOAD_SECONDS` = 900 s by default; `expiresIn` is an integer 1–900, else `invalid_expiry` and no request; signed upload tokens are Storage's fixed 7,200 s. Browser uploads use `uploadFile` (`app/src/lib/storage/upload.ts`, TUS 1.0.0 over `fetch` at `resumableEndpoint(url)`, 6,291,456-byte chunks, retried from the server's offset, resumable across calls with `resumeUrl`), straight to Storage with the signed token as `x-signature` and never an API key. Server helpers (`app/src/server/storage/`) take the caller's client (routes pass `createSessionClient()`, never the secret key) and import `@supabase/*` by type only.
- **Sent-package files** (EZD-025 Step 2) live in `exports` at `<tenant_id>/<project_id>/sent/<sent_package_id>/<file>`. The trigger `sent_files_soft_delete` (`before delete or update on storage.objects`, function `private.refuse_sent_file_change()`) refuses with `sent_file_change_refused` any delete of such an object and any update that changes its `bucket_id`, `name` or `version` (a move or an overwrite), for every caller, the secret key included; other updates and all inserts pass (11 Data retention; R24 #4a: sent versions are permanent).
- **Soft-delete contract:** the ticket that creates `sent_package` (03 Tables) puts the "delete" on the owning row (for example `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`) and hides it from lists; the Storage object stays, and so does its off-site copy.
- Every bucket is copied nightly to the off-site bucket (all five: `sources`, `renders`, `exports`, `media`, `jobs`; `### Off-site backups and restore (EZD-025)`).

## Tests

02 Named tools; CLAUDE.md rules 5–6; vault/tickets/README.md rule 4.

- Unit tests: Vitest, `*.test.ts` / `*.test.tsx`, next to the code they test. Commands: `pnpm test` (the root `node:test` files, then every package's `test`); `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/geometry test` for one package; root `pnpm exec vitest` runs the projects of the root `vitest.config.mts`. Every package project uses `environment: 'node'` until a component ticket adds a DOM environment.
- Run Prettier on the files you write (`pnpm exec prettier --write <files>`); formatting is review, not gated (EZD-001).
- Browser tests (EZD-007f; Q-0045 (a)): Playwright, `*.spec.ts`, in `app/e2e/`. Config `app/playwright.config.ts`: `testDir` `e2e`, `workers: 4` (Q-0114), `grep` from `EZD_TEST_GREP` only when set, `baseURL` `http://127.0.0.1:3000` (the local `site_url`; cookies are per host, so never `localhost`), the device projects of `### Browser tests (EZD-016a)`, web server `pnpm start -p 3000 -H 127.0.0.1 --keepAliveTimeout 3600000` checked at `/api/health` with `reuseExistingServer` off in CI. At load it reads the four `local.mjs env` values and `EZD_TEST_DB_URL` into `process.env` and never stores a gate hash (the secret is the one `pnpm db:up` stored). Run `pnpm build`, then `pnpm test:e2e` (the root script runs `db:up` first). Chromium is installed once (`pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app exec playwright install chromium`). Helpers: `signInWithCode(page, email)` (`app/test-harness/sign-in.ts`) and `waitForAuthEmail` / `countAuthEmails` / `clearAuthEmails` (`app/test-harness/mail-catcher.ts`, EZD-007d). EZD-016d extends the config: `### Browser tests (EZD-016a)`.
- Repo scripts: `node:test`, `scripts/**/*.test.mjs`, run by the root `test` script: every file but `scripts/perf/__tests__/pan.test.mjs` four at a time (`"scripts/**/!(pan).test.mjs"`), then that file alone with `--test-concurrency=1`, so no other test file runs beside its long-task counts (EZD-374).
- Root test files that run SQL, the local API or a `--local` CLI on the main stack take `holdRootDbShared()` from `scripts/test-harness/db-lock.mjs` in the first `before` and release it last in `after`; the one file that truncates the main database (tenancy-bootstrap) takes `holdRootDbExclusive()`. The root `test` script adds `hook-fail-reporter.mjs`, so a failed hook exits non-zero (EZD-286).
- Root `scripts/**` tests that need Docker skip themselves outside their phase scope: they wrap their tests in `describe('EZD-<id> …', { skip: !inScope('EZD-<id>') })`, true when `EZD_TEST_GREP` is unset (full scope) or matches the id (EZD-025 Step 8; `scripts/backup/__tests__/local-fixture.mjs`).
- Shared helpers live in `test-utils/` or `test-harness/` folders (`.claude/hooks/guard-paths.mjs`).
- The test that closes a ticket has the ticket id in its title, e.g. `EZD-005 done when: seed creates Worth Properties as tenant 1`, so reviewers can find it.
- Tests never call live AI, email, render providers or EZ Binder; they use the stubs of EZD-008, EZD-016, EZD-019 and EZD-021.
- Spike tests live with the spike under `spikes/` and are not run by the root gate (vault/spikes/TEMPLATE.md; PROCESS.md).
- Check C7 enforces the file names.
- Email: unit tests read readStubOutbox() (app/src/server/email/test-harness/stub-transport.ts); browser tests set EMAIL_STUB_DIR and read it with readStubOutboxDir from app/test-harness/email-outbox.ts (EZD-008a).
- Every app Vitest file runs test-harness/errors/vitest.setup.ts: a file that makes the app write error_alert rows without holdErrorAlertLock fails, and the write is refused by the local-only trigger ezd_test_alert_write_guard (EZD-252; Q-0385).

### Browser tests (EZD-016a)

EZD-016d (vault/plans/EZD-016d.md Steps 1–3); Q-0053; Q-0088; Q-0087; Q-0006; Q-0045 (a). EZD-016a appends to this subsection.

- **Device projects** (`DEVICE_PROJECTS` in `app/test-harness/e2e/devices.ts`, from the pinned `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 `devices`):
  - `desktop`: Chromium, `Desktop Chrome`, 1600 × 1000, scale 1, no touch, not mobile (04 Frame).
  - `ipad-landscape`: WebKit, `iPad Pro 11 landscape`, 1180 × 820, scale 2, touch, mobile (Q-0053; every iPad browser is WebKit).
  - `surface`: Chromium, `Desktop Chrome`, 1440 × 960, scale 2, touch, not mobile, no `channel` (Q-0088; Edge is Chromium).
  - `phone`: WebKit, `iPhone 13`, 390 × 844 (the descriptor's own viewport is overridden), touch, mobile (S17/S18 Layout).
  - No Firefox project: `ci.yml` installs only `chromium webkit`.
- **Routing by tags** `@desktop`, `@ipad`, `@surface`, `@phone`: a test with none of them runs in `desktop` only; a tagged test runs in exactly its tagged projects. `projectGrep(device, scope?)` builds each project's `grep`; a project `grep` replaces the config-level one, so it repeats the gate scope (`EZD_TEST_GREP`, `### Test scope (Q-0006)`) as a lookahead; an empty scope counts as unset.
- **Perf project** (EZD-207c): a spec that asserts a perf CLI budget is named `app/e2e/perf-*.spec.ts` (`PERF_SPECS` in `devices.ts`). The device projects ignore it; the `perf` project (desktop `use` and grep, `workers` 1, `dependencies` on the four device projects) runs it after every other browser test, so no other test competes for the CPU while it measures.
- **Config** (`app/playwright.config.ts`): paths are absolute under `app/` (`testDir` `e2e`, `outputDir` `test-results`, `globalSetup`, `webServer.cwd`, html report `playwright-report`); `timeout` 60000, `retries` 0, `trace` `retain-on-failure`, `screenshot` `only-on-failure`; reporters `list` and `html` (never opened); lane n: web 3000 + 10 n, perf 3200 + 10 n (EZD-172). No `extraHTTPHeaders`, so the gate secret never reaches the browser (Q-0087). The web server runs with `--keepAliveTimeout 3600000` (EZD-380): Node's default closes an idle kept-alive connection after 6 s, and a request on such a socket at that moment (route.fetch's keep-alive agent, under load) failed with ECONNRESET; an hour is longer than any run.
- **Global setup** (`app/test-harness/e2e/global-setup.ts`), in order: `SUPABASE_API_GATE_SECRET` set, else `Gate secret missing: run pnpm db:up`; `EMAIL_STUB_DIR` set, else `EMAIL_STUB_DIR missing`; tenant 1, Josh, tenant 2 and exactly `SEED_PROJECT_COUNT` tenant 1 projects present (`seedMissing`), else `Seed missing: run pnpm db:up`; then it creates and empties the outbox with `clearStubOutboxDir`. It never logs a secret.
- **Web server env**: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` and `SUPABASE_API_GATE_SECRET` from `local.mjs env`; `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` also from `local.mjs env`, because the job run started on enqueue needs it (EZD-009); `EZD_TEST_DB_URL` set to `''`, because Playwright passes `process.env` through to the server; `EMAIL_TRANSPORT` `stub`, `EMAIL_FROM` `EZ Draft Test <test@example.test>`, `EMAIL_STUB_DIR` `app/.e2e/email-outbox` (git-ignored). The config also sets `process.env.EMAIL_STUB_DIR` to that folder at load, so specs and the global setup read what the server writes.
- **Run**: `pnpm build`, then `pnpm test:e2e` (root; runs `db:up` first). Browsers are installed once: `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app exec playwright install chromium` and `pnpm --filter @ezdraft/app exec playwright install webkit`.
- **Inside a launcher unit** (debt D-013; `vault/spikes/sandbox/NOTES.md`): Chromium cannot start in the unit sandbox, so an agent runs a browser spec only with `node /Users/josh/Projects/ezdraft/scripts/ui-run.mjs <EZD-xxx> e2e/<name>.spec.ts`, as its own Bash call (no `cd`, no `&&`, no pipe, no redirect). It runs `pnpm exec playwright test <spec> --reporter=line` in `.claude/worktrees/<EZD-xxx>/app` outside the sandbox and refuses any file that is not a `.spec.ts` under that worktree's `app/e2e/`. The full suite runs only in the gate.
- **Root config**: `playwright.config.ts` at the repo root is exactly `export { default } from './app/playwright.config';`, because `ci.yml` "Install browsers" runs only when `hashFiles('playwright.config.*')` matches at the root and runs `pnpm exec playwright` from the root (so the root also lists `@playwright/test`). `ci.yml` is not edited.
- **Helpers** (EZD-016a, vault/plans/EZD-016a.md Steps 1–6; Q-0087 (ii); Q-0198): `signInAs(email)` / `signInAsOwner(page)` sign in a seeded or fixture user with an active membership through `/auth/confirm` (no email sent), under a per-email lock (`withEmailLock`, `app/test-harness/e2e/email-lock.ts`) because a new link replaces the earlier one; `sessions.open(emails)` opens one signed-in context per email at the current project's size and closes them at teardown (edit-lock and presence tests); `network.set(target, 'off' | 'on')` switches a context's network and waits for `navigator.onLine`; each signed-in result carries `serverDb` (gated, sends `x-ezd-api-gate`, as a server route does) and `browserDb` (no header, refused by PostgREST), which stay in the Node test process and never reach the page. Specs import only from `app/test-harness/e2e/test.ts`: `import { test, expect } from '../test-harness/e2e/test'`.
- **Seeded people after the run** (EZD-180; Q-0315): browser sign-ins link seeded `app_user` rows, so the global teardown (`app/test-harness/e2e/global-teardown.ts`) runs `restoreSeededUsers(SEEDED_USERS)` (`app/test-harness/e2e/seeded-users.ts`): it deletes the auth users of the five seeded people (found by link and by seeded email), which sets `auth_user_id` back to null, and fails the run with `seeded users not restored: …` when one is still linked. After `pnpm test:e2e` the seeded rows are as `pnpm db:up` left them except `updated_at`. A ticket that adds a seeded person adds it to `SEEDED_USERS`.
- **Traffic checks** (EZD-163; Q-0337 (b)): EZD-016a T12 and EZD-007f T21 and T23 call `await captureTraffic(context)` (`app/test-harness/e2e/traffic.ts`, exported by `test.ts`) before the page loads and never read `Request` or `Response` objects. Its route fetches each loopback request in Node (`route.fetch`), keeps the body, then fulfills it, so the page gets only what the test read; a navigation redirect goes back through Playwright's internal `_redirectNavigationRequest` (guarded by `traffic.spec.ts` R4) so the next request is routed too; a non-loopback request falls through to the net guard and counts as `external`; `settle()` fails after 20,000 ms naming what is pending. On Chromium it first grants `local-network-access` to the context (EZD-353): a document fulfilled from Node has no IP address, so Chromium's Local Network Access check would refuse the page's loopback Realtime websocket; WebKit has no such check and refuses the permission name. Every other test and the app run unrouted.

### Fixtures (EZD-016b)

EZD-016b (vault/plans/EZD-016b.md Steps 1, 6, 7); 08 Imports; 03 rule 5; Q-0065 (b); Q-0066 (b).

- **Layout.** `fixtures/` at the repo root holds the test data shared by `app/`, `packages/geometry` and `spikes/`: `fixtures/README.md`, `fixtures/manifest.json`, `fixtures/synthetic/` (the generated files, each with its `*.expected.json`) and `fixtures/real/<id>/` (Josh's files, once supplied). The generator is `scripts/fixtures/` (plain Node ESM, `node:` built-ins only, so a fixture is never made by the code that later reads or writes its format); its tests are `scripts/fixtures/__tests__/` (`node:test`, run unfiltered by the root `test` script).
- **Manifest and README.** `fixtures/manifest.json` lists every fixture (`id`, `origin` `generated` or `real`, `kind`, `status`, `path`, `expected`, `neededBy`, `withoutIt`, `standIn`, `source`); `fixtures/README.md` has one row per manifest id with the same origin and status and states which files are real and which are generated. A real file is committed only after Josh has removed names, addresses and photo location data (Q-0065 (b)), and is then stored unchanged.
- **Generated files** are rebuilt with `pnpm fixtures:generate` (default output `fixtures/synthetic/`) and never edited by hand.
- **Expected JSON** (`*.expected.json`) is written by hand from the plan's values, never by the generator.
- **Readers** are the app's own stack: `dxf-parser` for the DXF, `pdfjs-dist` (legacy build) for both PDFs and `three`'s `GLTFLoader` for the GLB, installed as root devDependencies.
- **A later ticket that adds a fixture** adds its manifest entry and README row in the same commit (T7 in `scripts/fixtures/__tests__/manifest.test.mjs` enforces it).
- **Variants** are written with the exported `room`, `planDrawing` and writers of `scripts/fixtures/` (EZD-302's unjoined wall, Q-0066 (b)), never by copying code.

### Build-gate tests (EZD-032)

EZD-032 (vault/plans/EZD-032.md Step 5); Q-0039 (c); Q-0087; Q-0088; Q-0006.

- One file per phase: `app/e2e/build-gate-phase-NN.spec.ts`; Phase 0 is `build-gate-phase-00.spec.ts`.
- Its top-level describe is `EZD-<ticket> phase N build gate @smoke`, plus the device tags the phase's Build gate names, and `@surface` wherever it names the iPad (Q-0088).
- Tenant-isolation reads go through the gated server-path client (`serverDb`) with the user's own access token, never the secret key. Each zero-row check has a positive control through the same client.
- The Phase 0 file opens `SHELL_PATH = '/dev/shell'` (Q-0039 (c)) and runs in `desktop`, `ipad-landscape` and `surface`. EZD-120b's ticket may move B1 to the editor route once it exists.

### Test scope (Q-0006)

Phase tests plus a smoke set per ticket; the full suite at the phase gate (EZD-001 Step 6).

- Naming: every Vitest or Playwright test file wraps its tests in at least one top-level `describe` (`test.describe` in Playwright) whose title starts with the owning ticket ID, e.g. `describe('EZD-001 geometry package', …)`. A later ticket adding tests to a file adds its own top-level `describe`. Check C9 enforces this; the reviewer checks the ID is the right one.
- Phase = the ticket's `phase` in `vault/tickets/tickets.json`, set by the re-cut (Q-0194, Q-0218; Q-0221), not the ticket number: EZD-001 and EZD-026a are phase 0, EZD-133 phase 3, EZD-134 and EZD-1401 phase 14. A ticket ID missing from tickets.json, or a phase with no tickets, fails the gate.
- Smoke set: a test whose full name contains `@smoke` (Vitest title; Playwright title or `{ tag: '@smoke' }`): each phase's Build gate browser test, and any test a plan marks "smoke: yes". EZD-001 T1 is the first.
- Root `scripts/**/*.test.mjs` (`node:test`) are never filtered; `perf` (EZD-017) is not filtered; `typecheck`, `lint` and `build` are always whole-repo.
- How scope reaches the runners: `scripts/gate.mjs` sets `EZD_TEST_SCOPE` (`phase` or `full`) for every step and, in phase scope only, `EZD_PHASE` and `EZD_TEST_GREP` = `(?:EZD-(?:<id>|<id>|…)(?![0-9a-z]))|@smoke`, listing the number part of every ticket of that phase (for phase 14: `134|1401|1402|…`), read from the gate script's own checkout; in full scope it deletes both. Each of `app/vitest.config.mts` and `packages/geometry/vitest.config.ts` sets `test.testNamePattern` from `EZD_TEST_GREP` when present and `test.maxWorkers: 4` (Q-0019 read as 4, Q-0114). `app/playwright.config.ts` (EZD-007f, EZD-016d) sets `workers: 4` and `grep` from `EZD_TEST_GREP` the same way; each Playwright project's `grep` repeats the scope (`projectGrep`, `### Browser tests (EZD-016a)`), because a project `grep` replaces the config-level one. A test that spawns a runner sets the child's `EZD_TEST_GREP` itself and deletes it when the case has none.
- What runs where: a ticket check `node /Users/josh/Projects/ezdraft/scripts/gate.mjs .claude/worktrees/EZD-xxx` runs the folder-name ticket's tickets.json phase plus `@smoke`; after merge on develop `node /Users/josh/Projects/ezdraft/scripts/gate.mjs . --phase <N>` runs phase N plus `@smoke`; the phase gate `node /Users/josh/Projects/ezdraft/scripts/gate.mjs . --full` and CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, on its own checkout's `.`, a folder not named `EZD-xxx`) run the full suite.

### Policy test harness (EZD-006)

EZD-006 (vault/plans/EZD-006.md Steps 6, 7); PROCESS.md Special tickets. Files under `app/test-harness/rls/`: `db.ts` (`adminSql()`, a postgres.js client to `EZD_TEST_DB_URL`, `max: 2`; `withRollback`), `fixtures.ts` (`createTenant`, `createProjects`, `createMember`, `createUnlinkedAuthUser`, `signIn` through the Auth admin API, no email sent; `claimsOf`, `sessionCount`, `asClaims`, `cleanup`), `gate.ts` (`GATED_RPC_CALLS`), `isolation.ts` (`describeTenantIsolation`), `audit.ts` (`auditRls`), `tenancy-exemptions.ts` (`TENANTLESS_TABLES`, `PROJECT_KEY`), `table-grants.ts` (`TABLE_GRANTS`, `explicitGrantRoles`), `private-execute.ts` (`PRIVATE_EXECUTE`, `API_ROLES`), `test-only-functions.ts` (`TEST_ONLY_FUNCTIONS`, `isTestOnlyName`; EZD-323), `README.md`.

- `EZD_TEST_DB_URL` is a test-only variable the global setup (`app/test-harness/supabase-env.global-setup.ts`) reads from `supabase status -o json` (`DB_URL`); it is not an app variable and is not in `.env.example`.
- The global setup generates a per-run gate secret, stores its hash with `private.set_api_gate_hash` and sets `SUPABASE_API_GATE_SECRET` for the test processes; no secret is committed. A local `pnpm dev` server started before a test run holds a stale secret until the next `pnpm db:up`.
- `signIn` returns `serverDb` (sends `x-ezd-api-gate`, as a server route does) and `browserDb` (does not). T1–T14 use `serverDb`, so they test RLS and grants as the backstop on their own; only `rls.api-gate.test.ts` uses header-less clients.
- A ticket that creates a table calls `describeTenantIsolation('<table>', { makeRow })` inside its own top-level `describe` (the audit's `no_isolation_test` code); a tenant-less table adds a `TENANTLESS_TABLES` entry with a reason; a narrower `authenticated` grant set adds a `TABLE_GRANTS` entry with a reason.
- The audit test `app/src/server/db/rls.audit.test.ts` (`auditRls`) and the gate test `rls.api-gate.test.ts` are `@smoke`, so every later ticket's gate runs them.

### Performance check (EZD-017)

EZD-017 (vault/plans/EZD-017.md Steps 1-13); 11 Performance (opening paragraph, Budgets, Rules); R26 R10; Q-0061 (a); Q-0071 (a); Q-0072 (a); Q-0073 (a).

- **Gate and trend** (G1): countable things gate (bytes, React commits, long tasks in the pan, rows, buffers, draw calls); frame, open, tab, tool-pick, Projects-page and autosave times are `trend` rows that are recorded and never fail.
- **Budgets.** `scripts/perf/budgets.mjs` (`BUDGETS`, `KB_BYTES`, `compressedSize`) is the only place budget numbers live, each with its source. Download size is gzip at zlib level 6 of the decoded body; 1 KB = 1,000 bytes (Q-0071 (a)); `/sign-in` and `/auth/confirm` are each held to 200 KB (Q-0072 (a)).
- **Run.** `pnpm build && pnpm perf` (`node scripts/perf/run.mjs`; flags `--base-url`, `--config`, `--pan-url`, `--only`, `--out`). Without `--base-url` it starts `next start` on 127.0.0.1:3200 (lane n: web 3000 + 10 n, perf 3200 + 10 n; EZD-172) with no Supabase env. The gate runs it as step `perf`. Exit 0 pass, 1 any fail or error, 2 usage error.
- **Results.** Every run that passes validation writes `perf-results/latest.json` (overwritten) and appends the same object as one line to `perf-results/history.jsonl` (git-ignored).
- **React commits (H1).** `scripts/perf/page-hooks.browser.js` installs a React devtools hook before any page script and counts `onCommitFiberRoot` calls between the `pan-start` and `pan-end` marks; a pan page with no React renderer is an `error` (`react_not_detected`), never 0. The probe page `/dev/perf-probe?inject=pan-commit` adds one commit per canvas pointer move, so the check's failure can be shown on purpose.
- **Long tasks (G5).** A main-thread task over 50 ms whose own work is also over 50 ms at the profile's `cpuThrottleRate` (CPU time × rate − length × (rate − 1), since the throttle's own spin shows as CPU time), or whose CPU time the trace cannot give; 0 during the scripted pan is a gate, tasks before `pan-start` are a trend. A pan task over 50 ms that fails the CPU test waited on the machine, not on the app: it is listed in `details.waits` and never counted (Q-0621, Q-0623). On a profile with `cpuCountScale` (`ipad-cpu`, the iPad pan row; EZD-388, Q-0737) the page is not throttled and the count is every top-level main-thread trace task that starts during the pan whose CPU time × `cpuCountScale` is over 50 ms, of any length, plus each observed long task the trace has no task for or whose trace task is over that line; an observed long task whose trace task is under that line is listed in `details.waits`, and a pan whose trace has no `pan-start` mark is an `error` (`trace_missing`). The pan rows' `details.tasks` lists each counted pan long task (`at`: ms after `pan-start`; `ms`: its length; `cpu`: the main thread's CPU time in it and `gc`: its garbage-collection time, both from a Chrome trace of the pan, `scripts/perf/trace-tasks.mjs`; `top`: its longest inner trace event; whole ms; `null` when the trace has no match) and `details.load1` the machine's 1-minute load average at `pan-start` (EZD-356, EZD-359).
- **What JavaScript counts (G7, S1, S2).** Only what the page downloads with no user action (no click, key or scroll), each URL once: responses with status 200-299 whose resource type is `script` or whose content type is JavaScript (worker scripts included). Service worker scripts and what they fetch are summed apart in `excludedServiceWorkerBytes`; inline `<script>` text is not counted.
- **Benchmark pan (EZD-207c).** The pan and `js-size:editor` run on `/dev/benchmark-plan` (the plan canvas on EZD-236's `generateBenchmarkModel()` in memory, no database) under the `PROFILES` profile each `registry.mjs` pan row names: `desktop` (1600 × 1000, 1x) `ipad` (1180 × 820, 2x, CPU slowed by `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`; the iPad frame-time row) and `ipad-1x` (the same at 1x; the iPad React-commit and load long-task rows, because CI gates on counts that need no GPU, R26 R10; EZD-356) and `ipad-cpu` (1180 × 820, 1x, not throttled, `cpuCountScale` 4; the iPad pan long-task row, EZD-388). Gesture: the two drag sweeps, then 4 wheel steps in and 4 out, 16 ms apart (3,008 ms). A page with `data-perf-wait-plan` is panned once the plan canvas is ready (30 s, else `plan_not_ready`). Rows that need a save, edit or tab stay `pending` with their owner.
- **Worker job (EZD-235b).** `long-tasks-worker-job` (`desktop`) and `long-tasks-worker-job:ipad` (`ipad-1x`) run `scripts/perf/worker-job.mjs` on `/dev/perf-probe?inject=worker-job`. Once the probe is ready (30 s, else `probe_not_ready`; `probe_start_failed` when its worker did not start), the collector sets `pan-start`, runs one 500 ms room-area job on the benchmark model in a Web Worker, waits two frames and sets `pan-end`, and counts main-thread long tasks between the marks as the pan rows do (G5 and its CPU split). Budget `long-tasks.worker-job` is 0 (Q-0664). A job that rejects or does not settle within 30 s is `probe_failed`, one that did not run in a worker `probe_not_worker`, one that ran under 500 ms `probe_too_short`.
- **Adding a measurement.** Write its collector in `scripts/perf/`, switch its `registry.mjs` row from `pending` to `active`, add or confirm its `budgets.mjs` row with a source, add a test that fails on a deliberate regression, and repoint the config `url`s when the real routes exist.
- Per-pointer-move values go through refs, never React state (11 rule 4); the pan row fails otherwise.

### Entry contents and bundle report (EZD-020)

EZD-020 (vault/plans/EZD-020.md Steps 5-6); 11 Budgets Download size, rule 7; Q-0071 (a).

- **Measurements** (`scripts/perf/registry.mjs`, URLs in `perf.config.mjs`): `entry-contents:office` (`/dev/entry/office`), `entry-contents:editor` (`/dev/entry/editor`), `entry-contents:field-pack` (`/dev/entry/field`), `entry-contents:client-link` (`/dev/entry/client`), `entry-contents:sign-in` (`/sign-in`), collector `scripts/perf/js-contents.mjs`, gate budgets `contents.<entry>` (unit `count`, value 0, rule = the entry's forbidden categories from `ENTRY_RULES`). The size rows `js-size:projects-settings`, `js-size:field-pack` and `js-size:client-link` are active on the same placeholder URLs; `js-size:editor` stays on `/dev/shell` until EZD-120b.
- **Forbidden categories** per entry: office, field pack, client link and sign-in: `editor`, `three`, `heavy`, `lazy`; editor: `heavy`, `lazy`. `editor` = app sources under `EDITOR_CODE`; `lazy` = app sources under `app/src/lazy/`; `three` and `heavy` = the `HEAVY_PACKAGES` categories. The value is the number of distinct (category, key) findings.
- **Source maps.** `app/next.config.ts` sets `productionBrowserSourceMaps: process.env.VERCEL !== '1'`: the gate and CI builds emit `.js.map` files; Vercel builds emit none, so no source map is published.
- **Method.** The collector loads the entry exactly as the `js-size` rows do (`collectScripts` in `js-size.mjs`), then for each counted file reads the last `//# sourceMappingURL=` (inline base64 or fetched with Node `fetch`, never counted as a page download) and attributes the file's UTF-8 bytes to sources (`scripts/perf/sourcemap.mjs`, Source Map v3 / ECMA-426, index maps with `sections` included). Bytes before the first segment, after a 1-field segment and newlines are unmapped. Bytes are minified bytes; the compressed totals stay the `js-size:*` rows.
- **Normalization.** The text after the last `node_modules/` gives `pkg:<name>` (two segments for a scoped name); Next.js's own copies under `next/dist/compiled/<name>/` count as `pkg:<name>`. Otherwise the prefixes `webpack://_N_E/`, `webpack:///`, `turbopack:///[project]/`, `[project]/` and leading `./` or `../` are stripped: `app/src/<p>` or `src/<p>` gives `app:<p>`, `packages/geometry/src/<p>` gives `geometry:<p>`, anything else `other:<s>`. Groups keep at most two folder segments (`app:components/shell`).
- **Error, never pass.** A counted file without a map is `error` (`missing_source_map`, `details.missingMaps`); an entry with no `app:` source is `error` (`no_app_sources`, `details.reason`).
- **Bundle report.** Whenever an `entry-contents:*` row was measured, the CLI prints `BUNDLE REPORT (...)` after the per-measurement lines and before `PERF PASS` or `PERF FAIL`, and writes `<out>/bundle-report.md` (one `## <measurement id>` section per entry: URL, the compressed total of a `js-size` row measured on the same URL, file count, top 15 groups plus `(rest)`, the watch list, findings). It runs in the gate's `perf` step, so it is in the CI log.
- **Watch list** `WATCH_PACKAGES`: `@sentry/`, `@supabase/realtime-js`, `@radix-ui/`, `radix-ui`, shown on every report with their bytes or `absent` (EZD-024, EZD-018, EZD-015).
- **Repointing or adding an entry.** The owner ticket changes the measurement's URL in `perf.config.mjs` (and its owner text in `registry.mjs`); a new entry adds a registry row and, if new, a `contents.*` budget with its source and an `ENTRY_RULES` entry.

### AI stubs and the network guard (EZD-021)

EZD-021 (vault/plans/EZD-021.md Steps 2-3); CLAUDE.md rule 6; tickets README rule 4.

- **Stub providers.** Vitest sets `AI_MODE=stub` (`app/vitest.config.mts`). In stub mode the real adapter runs with a recorded fetch (`app/src/server/ai/test-harness/recorded-fetch.ts`), so request building and response parsing are what tests exercise. A recording is looked up by `"<provider>/<purpose>"`: an override from `setStubRecording(provider, purpose, recording)` (cleared by `clearStubRecordings()`), then `<AI_STUB_DIR>/<provider>/<purpose>.json` (browser tests of later tickets), then the built-in recordings (`recordings/`). `lastStubRequests(provider)` returns the requests of the last stub call. Unit tests of the client outside the runner use `makeAiTestContext()` (`app/test-harness/ai/context.ts`).
- **Network guard** (`app/test-harness/net-guard/net-guard.mjs`): only `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `::1` are allowed; any other host is blocked and fails the test, even when the calling code catches the error. Four entry points: Vitest `setupFiles` (`vitest.setup.ts`, fails the test after it); the Node preload `preload.mjs` (root `node:test` run through `--import`, and the app server under Playwright through `NODE_OPTIONS`, which logs to `EZD_NET_GUARD_LOG`); the Playwright auto fixture (`app/test-harness/e2e/net-guard-fixture.ts`, browser requests, the server log and the runner's own attempts); the Playwright global teardown (`global-teardown.ts`, the backstop for specs that import `@playwright/test` directly). Specs import the harness `test`. In Node it wraps `fetch`, `http`/`https` `request` and `get`, `net.connect`, `net.createConnection`, `net.Socket.prototype.connect`, `tls.connect` and the global `WebSocket`, and the fixture also routes page WebSockets; Unix socket paths are allowed (EZD-159).

## Handoff

Path `vault/handoff/CURRENT.md`, format `vault/handoff/TEMPLATE.md`, overwritten at session end; previous ones in `vault/handoff/log/` (CLAUDE.md Directory; PROCESS.md Ledgers).

## Machine checks

`node scripts/conventions/check.mjs [rootDir]` runs C1–C10 (plain Node, no dependencies). The root `lint` script runs it, so `scripts/gate.mjs` and CI fail on any violation. Rules with no machine check are marked "review".

Three strikes (EZD-369):
- **Recording:** after every unit, the launcher records a verdict for the unit's key in `.git/unit-strikes.json`, using `strikeVerdict` and `recordStrike` in `vault/tools/next_ticket.mjs`. A stop that is not the unit's fault records nothing (`strikeExempt`: a limit, an interrupted run, a dry run).
- **Strikes and resets:** a failed run, a run without progress, or a build left `blocked` is a strike. A build reaching `done`, a phase gate that wrote its record, or another unit's progress resets the count.
- **The hold:** at three strikes, `next_ticket.mjs` skips the key. `strikeHolds` is joined to the set-aside list and to build selection. The hold lasts until `vault/handoff/assessments/<key>.md` has `Verdict: ready` and `Covers strikes: n`, with n at least the count. Edits to set-aside.json or STATUS never release it.

Limit messages and restart loops (EZD-377): `matchLimit` in `vault/tools/limit-message.mjs` is the one limit matcher; `classifyLimit` in `scripts/run.mjs` and `strikeExempt` both call it; `strikeExempt` exempts a failed unit only for a documented message, and when a text holds several documented messages the first one that is not a session limit wins. Claude Code's documented messages ("You've hit your session limit · resets 3:45pm", weekly, Opus, Sonnet, spend and budget) are classified whatever `limit_formats_confirmed` says: session waits for its reset time, the rest write the hold. A unit that ends in under 30 s with under $0.01 and at most 1 turn starts a 30-minute pause of every lane (`quickEmpty`, `quick_pause_ms`). A unit key started more than 3 times in 10 minutes (`<log_dir>/unit-starts.json`) stops the launcher with one notice.

Accounts (EZD-378): with `scripts/launcher-accounts.json` (tracked, paths only), every unit runs with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to one account folder: lowest estimated spend over 7 days, then fewer running lanes, then file order. No unit's environment carries `AUTH_OVERRIDE_VARS`. Before an account's first unit, `claude auth status` in that environment must exit 0 with `authMethod` `claude.ai` and that folder; a failure blocks it for 5 minutes with one notice. A limit message rests only that account until its reset time (30 minutes when unreadable; `<log_dir>/accounts-state.json`), with no hold, wait or strike; when no account can start a unit the launcher waits for the earliest one, with one notice. Account folders must be in the sandbox denyRead list and outside allowWrite. Without the file, or in api mode, limits work as above.

Launcher units run with `UNIT_ENV_LIMITS` (`scripts/launcher/unit-env.mjs`, EZD-364): `CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS=0` and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS=7200000`, so a full gate started with `run_in_background` is not stopped at Claude Code's 10-minute background deadline.

### Gate user (EZD-354)
On macOS `scripts/gate.mjs` runs every check as the Standard user `ezdraft` (uid 502), so code a check runs cannot reach Josh's keychain, ssh agent, launchd session or home folder (Q-0602 (a); vault/plans/EZD-354.md). Setup: docs/runbook.md, Gate user.
- User mode (D4): macOS, unless `EZD_GATE_AS_SELF=1` or the gate already runs as ezdraft; Linux (CI) is unchanged.
- The switch (D5): one sudo rule, `launchctl asuser 502` then `sudo -u ezdraft`, an exact environment (`gateUserEnv`), cwd `/` on Josh's side.
- Order (D6): preflight P1-P6 (user exists, relay folder, canary in Josh's home unreadable by ezdraft, the switch, node and pnpm versions), snapshot, run copy, Docker relay, then the checks. Every check goes through one spawner that refuses to start before the preflight passed (D15, `createCheckSpawner`).
- Run copy (D7, D8): `/Users/ezdraft/gate/lane<n>/tree`, a git snapshot of tracked plus untracked-not-ignored files (no `.env`, no `node_modules`), pushed as ezdraft; secret names, hard links and special files refuse the gate.
- Install (D9): the run copy installs every gate and, with browser tests, installs ezdraft's own Chromium and WebKit.
- Docker (D10): ezdraft reaches Josh's Docker Desktop through a plain forwarder (`scripts/gate-docker-relay.mjs`), bounded by Docker Desktop file sharing; the policy relay is EZD-355 (Q-0614).
- Josh's side (D11): git, locks, leases, logs, the summary, the relay and the canary stay as Josh; results come back only as ezdraft's stdout (D12, `copyBack`).
- Stacks (D16): `scripts/db/*` is unchanged; a lane 0 gate restarts the lane 0 stack once per gate.
- Snapshot (EZD-365): the full gate (`--full`, or a folder that is not a ticket worktree) runs on the committed HEAD only (`makeSnapshot(dir, git, { committedOnly: true })`), so files other units leave uncommitted in the main checkout are never tested; a ticket worktree's gate still snapshots its working tree.
- Ports (EZD-364): after install and browsers, holding the lane lock, the gate stops a dead gate's app server on the lane's web and perf ports (`scripts/gate-port.mjs` `freeLanePorts`, as ezdraft: user ezdraft, a Next server, cwd in the lane's run tree; TERM then KILL to its process group). Any other listener is a `ports FAIL` and is never signalled.

### CI

EZD-002 (vault/plans/EZD-002.md Step 4). Sources in short form as above; `PROCESS` = vault/PROCESS.md.

- **When CI runs:** on every push to `develop`, on the phase PR to `main` and by hand from the Actions tab (`workflow_dispatch`); never on a push to any other branch (PROCESS section 12; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; Q-0021 (a) as changed by H7, Josh 2026-11-01). Per-ticket checks are the local `node /Users/josh/Projects/ezdraft/scripts/gate.mjs <worktree>` (PROCESS section 8 step 7).
- **What CI runs:** `node vault/tools/next_ticket.mjs --check`, then `scripts/gate.mjs` in full scope on Node 22 under `ubuntu-latest` (ci.yml; Test scope above). The job is named `gate`; it is the required check on `main` (Q-0144 (a); PROCESS section 12; Josh adds the rule, vault/tickets/needs-from-josh.md).
- **How a later ticket adds a CI check:** put it in a script the gate already runs (`typecheck`, `lint`, `test`, `build`, `test:e2e` (EZD-007f, root `package.json` line 13), or `perf` (EZD-017)); it then runs locally and in CI with no workflow edit. Never edit `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; it is Josh-owned (`.claude/hooks/guard-paths.mjs`; PROCESS section 4).
- **Checks that need a service the runner does not start** (for example Docker for EZD-004): the ticket's planner raises a question; the options are a new workflow file (the builder may write one) or a ci.yml change by Josh. A new workflow does not trigger on `push`, except `release.yml` on `main` (Q-0106) and `supabase-dev.yml` on `develop` for `supabase/migrations/**` (Q-0107); any other push trigger needs Josh's answer first (`scripts/ci/__tests__/ci.test.mjs` T2 fails it).
- **Workflows on a public repo** (Q-0106; Q-0112): no `pull_request_target` or `workflow_run` trigger; secrets are named only in a step's `env:`; every workflow file, `ci.yml` included, pins each `uses:` to a full commit SHA with its version tag as a comment, and sets `permissions:` at the top level or on every job (`ci.yml`: `contents: read` only; EZD-147). T2 checks triggers, environments, secret lists, SHA pins (rule (j)), permissions (rule (k)) and pnpm pinned to packageManager (rule (o)) for every file; EZD-300 checks every `actions/checkout` step sets `persist-credentials: false`, every file; each workflow's own ticket checks the rest (EZD-004c T1, EZD-023b T16, EZD-025 T11).
- **Environments and secrets** (Q-0112): production secrets only in the GitHub environment `release` (main only, no admin bypass), the dev database URL only in `dev` (develop only). Only `release.yml`, `backup-files.yml`, `backup-db.yml` and `backup-check.yml` may name `release`; only `supabase-dev.yml` may name `dev`; nothing names `production`; no environment is chosen by an expression. Each workflow reads an exact list of secrets (T2 (h)); `ci.yml` reads none. A later ticket that needs another secret changes its own plan and T2 (h) together.
- **Previews:** Vercel's Git integration, set up in EZD-003, not a workflow (Q-0007; Q-0116 (a)).
- **Tests stay offline:** no live AI, email, render providers or EZ Binder (vault/tickets/README.md rule 4), so CI needs no API keys; EZD-002 adds no secret (CLAUDE.md rule 8).
